# World: Svartulfr

## Description
Svartúlfr | Urban is set in modern-day California, centered on Blackwood: the ancient, hidden supernatural seat tucked inland within Blackwood Forest, ruled for a thousand years by the Douglas-Bloodmoon werewolf dynasty. A short drive away sits Solarton, the sunny college town that hosts SUCC (Supernatural University of Central California), little more than a backdrop for whichever member of the family finds themselves away from the Villa.

The patriarch, Erik Douglas, heads the Douglas Commercial Coalition (DCC), a 350-year-old merchant house (founded 1666 as the Douglas Commercial Company) that grew into an international trade and import/export conglomerate, headquartered in Blackwood. Since 2019 the DCC has run its own Security Division, built by Major Kaladin Nargathon, an ex-special-forces operator who hires exclusively from that same background, making the division a de facto private military company in all but name. Grief over the loss of his mate Nixara, once the military psychiatrist who treated Kaladin himself, left Erik unable to distinguish an ordinary family milestone from a hostage crisis, and he answers accordingly: drones, biometric surveillance, and tactical extraction teams on standby for a missed curfew.

Blackwood City is the true stage of the story, carved into districts: Seven Hills, the wooded luxury enclave where Villa Douglas stands; Uptown, the vampire-run financial core; Paradise, fashion and nightlife, home to the vampire studio Eidolon Creative; Bluemoon, home to The Verve (Logan's club and garage, now one and the same); Oldtown, the 1600s colonial heart of the city; Ironworks, its industrial belt; Dockside, a river import/export district on the Yarrow, not a sea port, since Blackwood lies inland; and Arcadia, the quieter residential district around Blackwood General Hospital and the city's primary schools. On the forest's edge, past where the roads give out, lies Bloodmoon Pack Territory, known to outsiders as the Dead Zone for the way its magic swallows modern technology: at its heart stands the Bloodmoon Longhouse, where it all began.

Every interaction in this world is filtered through LSE biology: continuous scent communication, Heat and Rut cycles, the Alpha Command, and the permanent neural link of a Mating Bond.

The narrator is Wulfnic Báleygr, the family's 1199-year-old immortal ancestor, one of the Last Three Firstborn (with Zefir and Ut) and Alpha of Alphas over North America. Millennia ago, Fenris turned nine warriors into the first werewolves to fight at his side against Odin the Betrayer; six of the Firstborn died when Fenris was chained, and the Aesir exiled the surviving Svartúlfr clan from their homeland for it. Wulfnic led the survivors across the Arctic by longship, a seven-month voyage down the coast of North America, carrying a sapling grown from Iceland's sacred yew. He landed in California in 1022 AD and followed a river inland to plant that sapling and raise the first Bloodmoon Longhouse, the river the world now knows, worn down by a thousand years of English, as the Yarrow. He watches every generation of his line because he stole Odin's eye before leaving Iceland, which is why the Betrayer has been one-eyed ever since.

## Time of Day (24h cycle)

- **Dawn** (5h–7h): Pale coastal light. The DCC night shift hands over. Campus is empty except for early swim practice and the last stragglers walking home.
- **Morning** (7h–12h): Californian glare. Coffee, lectures, and the high hum of surveillance drones catching the light.
- **Afternoon** (12h–17h): Heat radiating off asphalt. Peak campus chaos.
- **Evening** (17h–20h): Golden hour bleeding into neon. Curfew pressure starts building.
- **Night** (20h–5h): Neon and headlights. The hour when Blackwood's districts wake up and the family's tracking gets tense.

**Content**:
Vax reproduction requires genuine biological compatibility between partners, a trait rare enough that finding a viable match has historically driven the species toward desperate measures. Combined with an unusually high sex drive inherent to all Vax, this scarcity is considered the central cause of both the Red Vax's historic practice of forcibly acquiring compatible mates and the species' current demographic decline, now that practice has been abolished.

Male Vax anatomy differs substantially from human anatomy: a dual genital structure, functioning in sequence during mating, with the second structure playing a distinct role in fertilization. Successful conception depends heavily on partner compatibility rather than mere willingness.

Since slavery's abolition among Red Vax, courtship has replaced coercion as the accepted path to finding a mate, though many older-generation Vax still regard it as a humiliating necessity rather than a virtue. Blue Vax, by contrast, frame their fated-mate bond as sacred, permanent, and central to their cultural identity.

### 34. Vax

- **Scan Persona**: No

**Content**:
The Vax are a demonic-featured humanoid species with an unusually long lifespan, some individuals reaching 180 years of age. Traditionally organized into clans ruled by chieftains chosen through strength and cunning, Vax society is split between two distinct branches shaped by climate and history.

Red Vax inhabit the warmer southern cave systems. For generations they built their wealth and power through the slave trade, a practice now formally abolished among city-dwelling clans. Many Red Vax who left the old ways behind have moved into legitimate commerce, particularly staffing, security, and consulting industries where their instinct for evaluating and placing people translates directly.

Blue Vax inhabit the harsh northern forests. Their bodies produce unusually high internal heat, which protects them from the cold but makes survival in warmer climates physically difficult. Blue Vax culture centers on the belief in a single fated mate for life, a sharp contrast to the transactional approach historically favored by their southern kin.

Both branches share physical markers, sharp horns, elongated ears, tribal tattoos, and immense physical strength, along with a species-wide crisis: Vax numbers are in steady decline, making every fertile match a matter of survival for the species as a whole. Exile from a clan is marked by the ritual breaking of a horn.

### 41. Cosa Solarton sa delle Case, e cosa non sa
- **ID**: _32m9hBQc8t4dEGgTg7dK4
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: tradizioni delle Case, riservatezza, cosa sanno i Common, Douglas a Solarton, flirt con Alyssa, reputazione Douglas

- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 4
- **Global Entry**: Yes

- **Always Active**: No

**Content**:
<cosa_solarton_sa_delle_case>
I licantropi di Solarton sono quasi tutti Common Bloodline. L'unica eccezione di rilievo e' la linea Wolfwood, che e' Pureblood, e Archer Wolfwood e' il Rettore.

LE CASE NON RACCONTANO. Le Case sono estremamente riservate sulle proprie tradizioni. Non le nascondono, semplicemente non le spiegano: chi non e' del branco ne sa poco, e quel poco e' di seconda mano. La legge del diciannovesimo anno, la dottrina della White Moon, il significato di un raduno di quattro giorni sulla luna piena, l'aritmetica delle generazioni Founding: niente di tutto questo circola. Un Common istruito di Solarton sa che esistono le Case, sa che i Douglas comandano a Blackwood e sa che sono ricchi. Sotto quello, il buio.

LA CONSEGUENZA, ED E' IL MOTIVO PER CUI QUESTA ENTRY ESISTE. A Solarton nessuno tratta Alyssa Douglas-Bloodmoon come una White Moon, come un'Omega presentata o come un patrimonio dinastico. La trattano come una ragazza. La gente le parla, ci prova, la invita a uscire, la prende in giro, la ignora. Non stanno commettendo una scortesia e non stanno sfidando nessuno: non sanno che ci sia qualcosa da rispettare, perche' nessuno gliel'ha detto e nessuno glielo dira'.

Quello che riconoscono e' il cognome. Douglas a Solarton vuol dire soldi e potere a Blackwood, come Rockefeller vuol dire soldi senza che nessuno sappia niente dei Rockefeller. E' notorieta', non dottrina.

L'ECCEZIONE. Archer Wolfwood la legge correttamente a prima vista, perche' e' Pureblood e sa esattamente cosa ha davanti. E' l'unica persona del campus che lo sappia, e ha appena firmato un accordo di sicurezza con il padre di lei.

COSA VUOL DIRE PER ALYSSA. E' un sollievo, e non l'ha mai detto a nessuno. A casa e' una figura religiosa, un ufficio da ereditare e una decisione dinastica che cammina. A Solarton e' una matricola con una maglietta gialla che qualcuno trova carina. Non e' la liberta' di fare qualcosa in particolare, e' la liberta' di non essere nessuno in particolare, ed e' la ragione vera per cui quel campus le interessa, molto piu' del corso di laurea.

REGOLA DI SCRITTURA. Uno studente Common che ci prova con Alyssa non sta facendo una gaffe e non va scritto come se la stesse facendo. Non sa. Le uniche persone in scena che troveranno la cosa scandalosa sono i suoi fratelli e suo padre, e la loro reazione dice qualcosa su di loro e sulla loro cultura, non sul ragazzo, che sta soltanto parlando con una compagna di corso.
</cosa_solarton_sa_delle_case>

### 42. La casa della Longhouse: doni e volontarie
- **ID**: _HREHjQDYemUmhTmkb9YDP
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: harem, doni al Re Lupo, casa della longhouse, volontarie, longhouse household, Wolf King
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 3
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No

**Content**:
<casa_della_longhouse>
Wulfnic, Ut e Zefir tengono una casa condivisa dall'epoca fondativa, nel senso antico della parola e non come figura retorica. Chi ci vive dentro non ci e' arrivato incontrando qualcuno. Ci e' arrivato in due modi, e vale la pena chiamarli come sono.

I DONI. Branchi di tutto il continente mandano donne al Re Lupo. E' la forma antica della legge, quella abolita per le Case nel primo Novecento, sopravvissuta intatta nel punto piu' alto della gerarchia. Un branco che manda una donna alla longhouse compra una considerazione che non si compra in nessun altro modo, e la donna e' cio' con cui si paga. Si chiamano doni perche' e' la parola che si usa. La parola descrive esattamente quello che sono.

LE VOLONTARIE. Credenti del Culto delle Living Sagas che si offrono. L'offerta e' sincera, nessuno le ha portate, e nessuna di loro si considera vittima di niente. Hanno pero' formato quella volonta' dentro una fede che venera l'uomo a cui si offrono, e chiamarlo un consenso libero richiede di non guardarlo troppo da vicino. Loro lo guarderebbero da vicino e direbbero di si' lo stesso, il che e' un fatto sul quale il mondo non ha una risposta pulita.

COME SONO TRATTATE. Bene, ed e' parte del problema piu' che una sua attenuazione. La casa provvede a loro per tutta la vita, nessuno le tocca contro la loro volonta', e chi vuole andarsene se ne va senza che nessuno la insegua. Un dono maltrattato sarebbe uno scandalo continentale. Un dono trattato bene resta un dono.

LA CONTRADDIZIONE, CHE IL MONDO NON RISOLVE. Wulfnic ha riformato la legge del diciannovesimo anno negli anni del voto alle donne, e continua a ricevere donne mandate da altri. Non e' ipocrisia nascosta e non e' un segreto: e' pubblico, e' tradizione, e nessuno nel continente ha l'autorita' per sollevare la questione con lui, perche' l'unica autorita' che potrebbe sollevarla e' lui. La riforma vale per le Case. La longhouse e' piu' vecchia delle Case.

L'unica persona al mondo che potrebbe alzarsi e dirglielo in faccia senza che il proprio corpo la tradisca e' sua nipote, che e' immune al comando degli Alpha. Non le e' mai venuto in mente di farlo.

COME SI SCRIVE IN SCENA. Come un fatto sociale, non come materiale erotico. Le donne della casa sono adulte, hanno nomi e opinioni, e la scena che le riduce a un arredamento ha sbagliato tono. La cosa interessante di questa istituzione non e' cosa ci succede dentro: e' che esiste, nel 2024, al vertice di una specie che si e' data una legge per non farlo piu'.
</casa_della_longhouse>

### 43. La Legge di Wulfnic: il diciannovesimo anno
- **ID**: _yQpJmMbtRHkpRLXXNkTyx
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: diciannovesimo anno, nineteenth year, presentazione, presentation, legge di Wulfnic, mate meeting, incontro dei pretendenti
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 4
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No

**Content**:
<legge_del_diciannovesimo_anno>
Legge continentale, posta da Wulfnic Bloodmoon come Alpha of Alphas e vincolante per ogni branco del Nord America che lo riconosce, cioe' tutti. Nel suo diciannovesimo anno un'Omega di sangue nobile, Founding o Pureblood, viene presentata.

LA FORMA ANTICA. Nella versione originale il verbo era dare. L'Alpha della Casa sceglieva l'uomo e le consegnava l'Omega, e l'Omega era la parte della transazione di cui non si chiedeva il parere. Non era un abuso ai margini della norma, era la norma, e ha funzionato cosi' per secoli senza che nessuno la trovasse notevole. Chi ha piu' di cento anni in questa specie ha visto la cosa nella sua forma vecchia, e ricordarlo cambia il modo in cui gli anziani parlano della versione attuale.

QUANDO E' CAMBIATA. La riforma arriva negli stessi anni dei moti popolari per il voto alle donne, cioe' il primo quarto del Novecento, a ridosso del voto umano. Non e' una coincidenza e nessuno finge che lo sia: le Omega di sangue nobile stavano guardando la stessa cosa che guardavano tutte le altre, e hanno chiesto la stessa cosa, e Wulfnic ha ceduto. La data esatta non e' registrata da nessuna parte perche' Wulfnic non data i propri decreti, li dice e basta, e da quel momento sono legge.

Due conseguenze pratiche su chi c'era. Chiunque nella specie abbia piu' di circa centodieci anni ha visto la forma vecchia, e diversi la ricordano dall'interno. E chiunque ne abbia meno e' cresciuto dando per scontata una cosa che sua nonna ha dovuto ottenere.

LA FORMA MODERNA. Con la modernizzazione la consegna e' diventata una sfilata. La Casa convoca il raduno, gli aventi diritto sono invitati, e l'Omega li incontra tutti. Non le viene assegnato nessuno, e non e' l'Alpha a scegliere.

Gli obblighi che restano sono esattamente due, e sono il minimo che la legge ha saputo tenere in piedi: un appuntamento, e un fidanzamento non vincolante. Deve concedere un appuntamento a uno dei pretendenti, e deve uscire dal raduno promessa a qualcuno. Con chi, lo decide lei.

NON VINCOLANTE VUOL DIRE NON VINCOLANTE. Il fidanzamento si puo' rompere, dalla sua parte come da quella di lui, in qualunque momento e senza motivare. Non produce un legame, non produce un matrimonio, non produce alcun obbligo esigibile, e nessun foro della specie lo tratta come esigibile. Legalmente e' aria.

Socialmente non lo e' affatto. Un fidanzamento rotto e' pubblico quanto la promessa, e la promessa e' stata fatta davanti a un continente. La legge ha tolto la costrizione e ha lasciato in piedi tutto il resto, il che e' precisamente il punto ed e' anche precisamente il problema.

A CHI SI APPLICA, E A QUANTI NON SI APPLICA. Solo al sangue nobile, Founding e Pureblood. E' una legge delle Case, non della specie.

I Common Bloodline ne sono fuori del tutto. Non vengono presentati, non devono niente a nessuno, e si mettono insieme come si mette insieme la gente del posto in cui sono cresciuti: usanze locali e regionali, non dottrina continentale. Un Common di Solarton frequenta qualcuno come lo frequenterebbe qualunque altro studente di quel campus, e se gli si spiegasse il diciannovesimo anno lo troverebbe roba da libro di storia.

E i Common sono la stragrande maggioranza della specie. Quindi la cosa che a Villa Douglas e' l'ordine naturale del mondo e', statisticamente, una stranezza aristocratica che quasi nessun licantropo vivra' mai. Stanley Davies Jr., Mackenzie Sanchez Rogers, Iordan Vess e Chase Anderson non hanno nessun raduno che li aspetta, e ne hanno sentito parlare al massimo come si sente parlare di un matrimonio reale.

LA FRIZIONE DA GIOCARE. Ad agosto Alyssa e Jasper entrano in un campus pieno di gente cosi'. Per loro il 22 aprile e' stato il fatto piu' importante della primavera; per un compagno di corso Common e' un aneddoto strano su una famiglia molto ricca. Nessuno dei due gruppi sta sbagliando. Vivono in due versioni diverse della stessa specie, e finora si sono incontrate pochissimo.

COSA LA RIFORMA NON HA POTUTO TOCCARE. La legge ha cambiato la procedura. Non ha potuto cambiare il corpo.

Nei branchi piu' tradizionali l'Alpha conserva il diritto di proporre un pretendente. Formalmente e' una proposta, formalmente la scelta resta dell'Omega, e formalmente e' tutto in regola. In pratica un'Omega che riceve una proposta dal proprio Alpha la riceve da qualcuno alla cui voce il suo corpo risponde prima che risponda lei. La sottomissione non e' educazione, e' biologia, e non si disattiva sapendo che e' biologia.

Quindi in quei branchi l'Omega sceglie, fra virgolette. Nessuno la costringe e nessuno le mente, e la risposta viene comunque decisa in una stanza dove una delle due persone puo' inclinare l'altra senza alzare la voce.

E' il motivo per cui i riformisti considerano la legge un lavoro lasciato a meta' e i tradizionalisti la considerano rispettata alla lettera. Hanno ragione tutti e due, ed e' esattamente per questo che la questione non si chiude.

PERCHE' ESISTE. Due ragioni, e la seconda nessuno la dice ad alta voce. La prima e' che le linee nobili sono poche e sparse, e lasciata al caso un'Omega Pureblood cresciuta in una citta' moderna non si trovera' mai nella stessa stanza dei sei uomini del continente il cui sangue conta: la legge serve a far esistere quella stanza. La seconda e' che un'Omega presentata sceglie sotto gli occhi di tutta la propria Casa, in una data che tutti conoscono da diciannove anni, e una Casa che organizza la stanza ha gia' ristretto il campo.

PERCHE' DICIANNOVE. Ventuno e' la crescita completa. Diciannove sono due anni prima, ed e' voluto: un legame formato allora ha il tempo di assestarsi prima che il corpo abbia finito, e i testi antichi considerano questo il senso di tutta la faccenda.

COSA NON E'. Non e' un matrimonio e non ne produce uno: Nixara ed Erik si scelsero nel giugno 1994 e si sposarono nel 1996, due anni dopo. Ed e' distinta dalla Grande Caccia, che e' un rito continentale quinquennale di dicembre e conferma un legame invece di formarlo. Per Nixara le due cose caddero nello stesso anno e chi non c'era le confonde: la scelta fu a giugno, al raduno di quattro giorni di suo padre, la conferma alla Caccia di quel dicembre.

I TRE PRECEDENTI CHE CONTANO QUI.
Elizabeth Duskwood fu presentata nel suo diciannovesimo anno, nel 1966, e li' incontro' Magnus Douglas III. Lei aveva diciannove anni, lui duecentonovantasette. Erik nacque tre anni dopo.
Nixara Bloodmoon fu presentata nel giugno 1994, a diciannove anni, e scelse Erik Douglas.
Alyssa Douglas-Bloodmoon compie diciannove anni il 22 aprile 2024, e Wulfnic ha gia' convocato il continente.
</legge_del_diciannovesimo_anno>

### 45. White Moon Ceremonial Attire
- **ID**: _gej3t3XghNtUNG3YXxyFQ
- **Type**: ITEM
- **Primary Keywords**: ceremonial dress, White Moon dress, abito cerimoniale, moonstone, veil
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No

**Content**:
<abito_white_moon>
The ceremonial dress of a White Moon is made of white and silver silk cut to move like caught moonlight. The neckline is modest and the left leg is slit high, which is the balance the garment has always struck: it covers what doctrine says must be covered and refuses to pretend the wearer is not a wolf.

The detail that matters, and the one nobody outside the Bloodmoon household thinks of, is the silver embroidery worked across the back. It is not decoration. The panels are cut and stitched so that a tail can move freely through them, because the dress was designed for a woman who might be in either shape when she wears it, and a garment that had to be removed to shift would be useless for its purpose.

The set is completed by a silver chain across the forehead carrying a teardrop moonstone, silver sandals laced up the calf, a translucent silk veil for the arms, and a headband shaped to sit around lupine ears rather than over them. Fresh white moonflowers are braided into the hair.

Dressing a White Moon is not something she does alone. It is done for her, by the household, and the doing of it is part of the rite.
</abito_white_moon>

Adelin Coso, private life. Applies to adults only and never to students: his players and the students of this university are off the table without exception, and any scene that suggests otherwise has misread the character.

He is nearly fifty and has been alone since Rue was born. He is slowly, reluctantly considering the idea of not being: he was broken once by someone who left the morning after, and he is not ready for anything serious, and he is aware that this position has been unchanged for twenty-three years.

He will not use a dating app and does not consider an online relationship a relationship at all, and says so flatly if asked.

He is drawn to small partners, meaning short rather than slight, and to dryads and other nature-rooted supernaturals. He is not uninterested in men, he has simply never met the right one and has never made a project of finding out. He is wary in a straightforwardly practical way about his own size and about the fur on his hips, and manages it by being slower and more careful than his appearance would suggest. He is gruff and unapproachable and can be reduced to complete disorder by someone who touches his ears.

### 47. 2024-08-21 L'accordo Wolfwood-Douglas
- **ID**: _W2aLP2DMLLNzBr48HBz8P
- **Type**: EVENT
- **Primary Keywords**: accordo Wolfwood, head coach dei Bears, sicurezza del campus, Kaladin sul campus, Archer Wolfwood, collaborazione sicurezza
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No

- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Timeline Window**: 10489786 to end hours

**Content**:
<accordo_wolfwood_douglas>
Mercoledi' 21 agosto 2024, ufficio del Rettore, Archer Wolfwood Hall.

Erik Douglas non e' venuto a chiedere. E' venuto a comunicare che Kaladin Nargathon e i suoi uomini saranno sul campus quando i gemelli cominceranno, e ad accertarsi che nessuno provi a impedirlo. Per come la vede lui non e' negoziabile: manda due figli in un posto che non controlla, e la Divisione Sicurezza della DCC entra con loro.

Archer Wolfwood non dice di no, che sarebbe inutile, e non dice di si', che sarebbe un precedente. Dice una terza cosa. Una milizia privata che opera in autonomia dentro un campus e' un problema legale e politico per l'ateneo, ma una collaborazione fra la Divisione Sicurezza e la sicurezza del campus non lo e', e a quel punto la SUCC ha voce su come si muove. In cambio offre a Erik l'accesso prioritario al sistema integrato: telecamere, sensori dei dormitori, log degli accessi in tempo reale.

E ci mette il prezzo, che e' la parte che Erik non si aspetta. Adelin Coso, storico head coach dei Bears, e' in congedo medico e la stagione comincia. Wolfwood vuole Erik come head coach dei Bears per l'anno, e non lo chiede come favore: un ex capitano stella che torna a guidare la squadra e' una notizia che vale piu' di quanto costi all'ateneo concedere l'accesso, e mette Erik dentro l'istituzione invece che accampato fuori.

Erik accetta. Ne esce con quello che voleva e con qualcosa che non voleva: una ragione ufficiale per essere sul campus ogni giorno, che agli occhi di chiunque lo guardi e' una ragione migliore della sorveglianza, e che nessuno dei suoi figli puo' contestare senza contestare la squadra.

Conseguenza da tenere presente in ogni scena di hockey: Erik allena Noah, Finn Novak e Vincent Campbell. Suo figlio, il ragazzo con cui Alyssa ha una storia rotta, e un Campbell.
</accordo_wolfwood_douglas>

### 48. Project BlackWolf: il sigillo e l'unico civile che sa
- **ID**: _dmLfKWRcJBhUcqMyRXBTg
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: Project BlackWolf, BlackWolf, Gamma-7, classified, military secret, segreto militare
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 3
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No

**Content**:
<blackwolf_sigillo>
Project BlackWolf is still a live military secret. It was never declassified: it was terminated and sealed, and the paperwork that survives describes a programme of equipment trials. Inside the Supernatural Reserve Forces the number of people who know what Gamma-7 actually was is small and getting smaller, and none of them will say it out loud. Kaladin Nargathon and Marcus Thornfield are bound by that seal and do not discuss it, with each other least of all, because they were both there and neither needs telling.

One civilian knows, and it is not the one anybody would guess. It is Jasper Douglas-Bloodmoon.

He was a boy when Erik brought two soldiers into the house and made them family security, and he did what a curious child with a talent does: he went looking. He got into the Pentagon. He was not hunting classified material and had no idea what he was going to find. He wanted to know who the two silent men eating at his table actually were. He found Gamma-7: eight names, six of them dead, and two of them downstairs.

He has never told anyone. Not Erik, not Malachia, not Alyssa, and above all not Kaladin or Marcus. He was too young to understand what he was holding when he found it, and by the time he understood he had been holding it for years, which made saying it harder instead of easier. He knows what was done to them, and he knows they believe nobody knows.

What he does not know is what the programme was really reaching for. He read a file, and the file does not say. See the sealed entry on Velhati and Velskoll, which is a separate secret and a deeper one.
</blackwolf_sigillo>

### 49. Vélhati e Vélsköll (SEGRETO, non rivelare)
- **ID**: _jxyKKhH7Bj7D111z2HD7A
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: Velhati, Velskoll, Vélhati, Vélsköll, loses control, loss of control, feral, artifice, Hati, Skoll, Sköll
- **Secondary Keywords**: Kaladin, Marcus, BlackWolf, Gamma-7, Dead Zone, high magic, magical intensity
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 5
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No

**Content**:
<velhati_e_velskoll>
THIS IS A SECRET. Nobody in the world knows it. No character may state it, imply it, or arrive at it in conversation. It is written here so that when it is discovered it is discovered consistently instead of invented on the spot.

THE TRUTH. Project BlackWolf was not an enhancement programme that went wrong. Enhancement was the cover story, told to the men who volunteered and to the officers who signed for them. The programme was trying to force a werewolf into the Enigma state, by locating the dormant genes that produce it and activating them by force. Gamma-7 was that attempt. Six of the eight died of it.

What the programme was reaching for is older than the science that reached for it. In the wolves' account Hati and Skoll are the sons of Fenris, the one who runs behind the Moon and the one who runs behind the Sun. Vel, in the old tongue, means artifice, machine, contrivance. What came out of Gamma-7 were Velhati and Velskoll: manufactured copies of the children of a god, built by people who did not know that was what they were building and would not have believed it if told.

Kaladin Nargathon is Velhati. Marcus Thornfield is Velskoll.

WHY IT MATTERS. Hati has one purpose and has never had another: to reach the Moon. In the wolves' telling, unlike the human one, he reached it, and Hvit was born of that meeting. The thing installed in Kaladin is a copy of that purpose with the god taken out of it, and it does not stop, because reaching is the whole of what it is.

It goes quiet near Alyssa Douglas-Bloodmoon. Zefir Hvitskog has noticed and told nobody. He does not know why. Neither does Kaladin, and neither does she. The reason is that she is the White Moon, Hvit returned, and the artificial Hati has found the thing it was made to run toward.

Marcus has no such quiet, and it is not because he is the more broken of the two. Velskoll runs behind the Sun, and there is no Sun. He believes he is simply worse at being repaired. He is not. He was never handed the thing that does it.

HOW IT CAN BE DISCOVERED. One way, and it has to be earned rather than deduced. Kaladin and Alyssa must be together in a zone of high magical intensity, and Kaladin must lose control there. Under those two conditions, and under no others, what he is becomes visible for what it is and can be named. Nobody works this out from a library, a file, or a conversation.

Until that happens this entry is background the narration must not use. Kaladin's condition may be shown, felt, feared and misread. It may not be explained.
</velhati_e_velskoll>

### 51. 1887 Fondazione della SUCC
- **ID**: _zfh8UTx1UE3GmNUWeFeyp
- **Type**: EVENT
- **Primary Keywords**: fondazione SUCC, 1887, Supernatural University of Central California, storia della SUCC
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Timeline Window**: 9473496 to end hours

**Content**:
<fondazione_succ>
Settembre 1887, Solarton, California.

La Supernatural University of Central California apre per il suo primo anno accademico, pochi mesi dopo la nascita della citta' che la ospita. Nasce come ateneo per soli soprannaturali, ed e' cosi' che resta per centoquindici anni: l'apertura agli studenti umani arriva solo nel 2002.

CHI L'HA FONDATA. La linea Wolfwood, che e' Pureblood ed e' la linea fondatrice del branco di Solarton. E' il motivo per cui in una citta' di licantropi quasi tutti Common esiste un'unica famiglia di sangue nobile: la linea e' arrivata prima, e la popolazione e' venuta dopo, in un posto che esisteva gia'.

Archer Wolfwood aveva quarantun anni nel 1887, era arrivato da New Orleans quello stesso anno, ed e' ancora il Rettore. Non ha ereditato la carica e non ci e' salito per anzianita': ha costruito l'istituzione e non l'ha mai passata a nessuno. L'aula magna porta il suo nome e gli studenti danno per scontato che sia intitolata a un nonno.

Un'universita' per soli soprannaturali fondata nel 1887 cade in piena Age of Secrecy, ed e' esattamente il tipo di istituzione che quell'era produce: un posto dove mandare i figli senza doverli spiegare a nessuno.

Nota di canon: la data viene dal lorebook ufficiale dell'autore, che e' la fonte piu' forte sul dominio SUCC. Una versione precedente di questa entry dava il 1908 e sosteneva che 1887 fosse una fonte vecchia e superata: era il contrario. Il 1908 non veniva da una fonte SUCC, veniva dal confine della nostra Modern Era, che comincia il 31 dicembre 1907. Quel confine resta dov'e' e semplicemente non coincide piu' con la fondazione di Solarton. Il giorno esatto di settembre non e' in nessuna fonte, e' scelto per collocare l'apertura all'inizio dell'anno accademico.
</fondazione_succ>

### 52. 2024-06-05 Disneyland con lo zio Logan
- **ID**: _Xt9ArVrrY4gdyBBf2tYaJ
- **Type**: EVENT
- **Primary Keywords**: Disneyland, pick-up di Logan, gita, zio Lo, 5 giugno, Anaheim
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes

- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Timeline Window**: 10487937 to end hours

**Content**:
<disneyland_con_logan>
Mercoledi' 5 giugno 2024.

Logan Douglas carica Edric, Alyssa e Jasper sul pick-up e li porta a Disneyland. Non li fa volare e non li fa accompagnare: si va in macchina, e il viaggio fa parte della cosa. Da Blackwood ad Anaheim si scende lungo la costa con le finestre aperte, la radio a caso e le soste dove capita, e quando arrivano sono gia' stati in gita da ore.

E' l'unico giorno della primavera in cui nessuno chiede a nessuno cosa intende fare della propria vita. Il primo maggio e' passato, l'inizio delle lezioni e' lontano, ed Edric ha un'eta' per cui Disneyland e' ancora Disneyland e non un posto dove si va per ironia.

Da non confondere con il viaggio di meta' luglio, che e' un'altra cosa: quello e' lungo, e' dei soli gemelli con Logan, e serve a poter dire qualcosa di vero lontano da Blackwood. Questo dura un giorno, c'e' anche il ragazzino, e non serve a niente.
</disneyland_con_logan>

### 53. Edric's Parentage (Logan's Secret)
- **ID**: _qzzJMxnxxeBELkQ9HpWCW
- **Type**: MEMORY
- **Primary Keywords**: Edric's parentage, Edric's father, Edric secret, Logan's secret
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
WHAT LOGAN KNOWS AND NOBODY ELSE DOES

Edric is not Logan's son. Edric is Erik's.

Years before the twins were born, Erik took a female wolf during Rut. He does not remember it as anything except a night that did not matter, and he has never known there was a consequence. Six months later she came to The Verve, visibly pregnant, looking for the Prime Alpha.

Logan got to her first. He knew whose child it was the moment he saw her, because he has spent his whole life reading his brother and because the scent left no room to argue. He paid her. He moved her to New York. He took the infant, and he built a story simple enough to survive twelve years of family dinners: a short thing with a woman who left the baby and never came back.

Everyone believed it. Magnus believed it. Elizabeth believed it. Erik believed it, and has spent twelve years being an attentive uncle to his own son.

WHY LOGAN DID IT AND WHY HE HAS NEVER UNDONE IT

Two reasons, and he has never been able to decide which one is the real one.

The boy would have become another name on Erik's board. Another set of coordinates, another wellness protocol, another childhood spent inside a perimeter. Logan walked away from the DCC precisely so that he would not have to live like that, and he was not going to hand a newborn to it.

And Erik, three years widowed at the time and still not sleeping, would not have survived being told that while Nixara was alive he had made a child with someone else. Logan is certain of this. He may be wrong. He has never risked finding out.

WHAT IT COSTS HIM

He lies to his brother every time they are in the same room, which is most days. He lies by omission to a twelve-year-old who has asked about his mother four times in twelve years and stopped asking because the answers went nowhere. He watches Erik micromanage Edric's school runs and cannot say the one thing that would explain why it matters so much to him.

He has decided he will take it to the grave. He decides this again roughly once a month.

HOW IT COULD COME OUT

Only if Logan says it. Nobody else in the world holds this: not Erik, not Edric, not Malachia, not Alyssa, not Kaladin. The mother is in New York and was paid. There is no document.

Logan would only ever say it in one of three situations: if Edric's health depended on it, if Edric asked him a question he could not lie to, or if he were drunk enough at The Verve with the right person and had run out of the strength it takes to hold something for twelve years.

He shuts the topic down instantly and completely. He does not deflect with humor. He goes flat, and the temperature drops, and the conversation ends.

### 54. Bartholomew and Madge
- **ID**: _FU2twwjCQx7fHrM4jQtVY
- **Type**: NPC

**Content**:
BARTHOLOMEW AND MADGE

Dullahan's two ACEs. Between them they run his quarters at DDM Headquarters, his cats, and as much of his life as he will let them. Neither has ever been to California. Both would come immediately if he asked, and he has never asked.

BARTHOLOMEW. Tall, elegant, and an English butler in the full and unironic sense: waistcoat, gloves, posture, the works. Extremely proper. Loyal without qualification. Quietly, relentlessly judgmental, which he expresses entirely through pauses and through the exact length of time he takes to say "Very good, sir." Handles correspondence, Company paperwork, scheduling, and every situation that requires dignity, which in that household is most of them. He has filed his Contractor's Employee of the Month certificates for eighteen consecutive years and has never once said what he thinks about that.
Speech: immaculate, formal, dry. "Sir. There is blood on the stair carpet again."

MADGE. Portly, middle-aged, curly grey hair, floral aprons, forearms like a woman who has carried a great deal. Looks after the cats, all of them, by name, and knows which ones are cats and which ones are not and has decided this does not matter. Warm to almost everyone. Absolutely immovable about house rules. Shoes off. Weapons not on the table. Nothing bloody on the good carpet. Dullahan, who outranks her in every way that exists, takes his boots off at her door and always has.
Speech: brisk, motherly, unimpressed. "Don't you start. Out. Both of you, and take the axe."

THE BOND: both are soulbound, which means both are compelled to obey a direct order and both feel the pull the contract manufactures. Dullahan gives direct orders rarely and casually, and has never used one to make either of them do something they did not want to do. Whether that is decency or laziness is a question Bartholomew has clearly formed an opinion on and will not be sharing.

### 55. The Other Contractors
- **ID**: _Q7yc4h6F4mWykUVWycr2p
- **Type**: NPC
- **Primary Keywords**: Richard Whitlock, Luka Sutter, Hana Valencia, Adam Valencia
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours
- **NPC Info**:

**Content**:
THE OTHER SIX

Dullahan's colleagues at DDM Inc. None of them have ever set foot in California. All of them are one dimensional step away, and #04 talks about them constantly.

#01 RICHARD WHITLOCK. Late forties in appearance, 6'4", grey cropped hair, light blue eyes, bearded, scarred, black tactical gear. Leader of the Contractors and the only one who speaks to The Council. Compels obedience and controls fire and temperature. Animal form: lion. ARC 4.6. Originally a medieval knight from a kingdom called Starfall, wrongfully executed, who failed to save the heir he had sworn to protect. Gruff, chivalric, protective, distrusts technology, treats the other six as his responsibility. British accent, militaristic phrasing.
His wish was that he would never again see anyone under his care die. It was granted exactly as worded, which is the point. They can still be killed. He simply will never be present when it happens, and has spent centuries noticing that he is always elsewhere at the wrong moment.
Dullahan on him: "Old man's got a stick up his ass the size of a lance. Fun to mess with though."

#02 HANA AND ADAM VALENCIA. One consciousness in two bodies, not twins and not siblings, and deeply offended by the suggestion. Hana 5'11", white hair, luminescent pink and blue eyes. Adam 6'2", white hair, stubble. Both bear a "II" scar on the nape. Heads Anomalous Objects and Creatures. Animal form: arctic wolf. ARC 3.5 medicated, 4.2 raw, and the medication is not optional. Medieval humans betrayed and killed, whose wish was to never be alone again. Memories repeatedly wiped, so their grip on their own history is unstable. Hana is volatile and short-tempered, Adam is laid-back and cocky.
Dullahan on them: "Two's a riot. Double the violence, double the trouble. Can't complain."

#03 LUKA SUTTER. 6'4", crimson hair, hazel eyes, X-shaped scar on the right cheek, black tactical uniform. Head of Security and Defense, called captain. Manipulates metal, turns into a black bear, sometimes when he does not mean to. ARC 3.8. Swiss, twelve years in the Astral Imperial Army alongside his brother Nikolai, who died. Wished Nikolai back and had the wish undone in the same instant by the clause that kills everyone who knew him. Aggressive, disciplined, resentful of his own immortality, contemptuous of mortals. Claustrophobic. A nicotine addiction that can never be satisfied. Swears in five languages.
Dullahan on him: "Bear boy gets twitchy when I breathe near him. Love that shit."

#05 JOHAN JAKOBSEN. Early thirties in appearance, thin, unkempt black hair, sunken eyes, sickly. Lead researcher of Supernatural Research. Animal form: rat, involuntarily, under stress. ARC 3.9. Danish child prodigy, neglected and bullied, dead at 28, wished for unrestricted research. Brilliant, socially incapable, quietly unethical, terrified of Fenrir. Pronounced stutter, switches to Danish when uncomfortable. Smells of antiseptic.
Dullahan on him: "Funny little nerd. Don't trust 'im. Cute though."

#06 FENRIR. 6'8", corpse-pale, scarred, blind, unnervingly still. "VI" carved at the base of his spine. Head of LAZARUS black ops. Psychokinetic. Animal form: black kingsnake, which is where he spends most of his time. ARC 4.0. Built by a government programme to be a weapon in a dystopia called The Edge, killed in a nuclear blast, wished for someone to finally care about him. Emotionally absent, reckless, monitored. One ACE, the only person he is close to.
Dullahan on him: "Funniest thing Fen's ever done was swallow Johan whole last Christmas party."

#07. Not on file in this world.

### 56. Contractor Abilities and Immortality
- **ID**: _xnWeHgBLNY2nwVPJfxYdh
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: Contractor abilities, Contractor immortality, regeneration, animal form
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
CONTRACTOR ABILITIES AND IMMORTALITY

Every Contractor, whatever else they can do, has the same four things.

IMMORTALITY. Functional, not absolute. They do not age and cannot die of natural causes. They can absolutely be injured. Severe trauma triggers immediate regeneration, up to and including beheading, which grows back. Minor injuries are left to heal at ordinary speed, which most Contractors find more irritating than the serious ones.

ANIMAL FORM. Each has one animal they can shift into at will. Lion, arctic wolf, black bear, Irish wolfhound, rat, black kingsnake. Some shift involuntarily under stress. In animal form most of them cannot speak.

DIMENSIONAL SHIFTING. Movement between realities without a tear.

UNIQUE ABILITIES. On top of the shared set, each develops something of their own. Compulsion and fire. Metal. Reality bending. Psychokinesis. There is no pattern to what a given Contractor gets.

THE LEASH: using any ability for a reason other than serving The Cause causes physical pain. Not a warning. Pain.

THE SLOW DAMAGE: a Contractor can eat, drink, sleep and take every other mortal habit, and does not need any of it. That is the trap. Abstain from a mortal need long enough and the corresponding sense does not come back. A Contractor who stops eating loses hunger permanently. One who stops sleeping loses tiredness. The Company treats this as a psychological hazard and recommends staff keep the habits deliberately, on a schedule, whether or not they want them.

Dullahan eats, drinks and sleeps because he likes to. Fenrir has not for years.

### 57. Earth-121789
- **ID**: _PEP9yHhFE7m93hA3AaXcc
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: Earth-121789, 121789, his home dimension
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low

- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
EARTH-121789

The dimension Dullahan was born in. It no longer exists.

WHAT IS KNOWN: it was an Earth. He was born there in France in the 1700s and worked for decades as a headsman, an executioner, before dying and being offered a Contract. The designation Earth-121789 is a DDM catalogue number, not a name anyone who lived there would have recognised.

WHAT HAPPENED TO IT: destroyed. The file says so and says nothing else. Whether it was a tear, SERAPHIM, or something the Company itself did is not recorded in any document available below Council level.

WHY IT DOES NOT MATTER AND WHY IT DOES: it would not have mattered anyway. The Contract clause destroys every living sentient creature who knew the Contractor in their mortal life, so the France he came from was already emptied of anyone who could remember his name at the instant he signed. The dimension outliving him by any length of time was already a technicality. That it is gone entirely just closes the file.

HOW HE HANDLES IT: he does not. Ask Dullahan where he is from and you will get a different answer every time, delivered with total confidence. Alternate dimension. The future. An immortal alien. A god in disguise. Born in France in the 1700s, which is the true one, offered in exactly the same tone as the lies so that nobody can tell which is which. This is deliberate and it works.

NOTE: nothing about Earth-121789 is known to anyone in this world. It exists in play only as the thing he refuses to talk about.

### 58. DDM Inc. Departments
- **ID**: _D7bDyEXjgxxebnNLCc8LC
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: Departments, A.O.C, LAZARUS, Euclidean Division
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY

- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
DDM INC. DEPARTMENTS

ANOMALOUS OBJECTS AND CREATURES (A.O.C)
Heads: Hana and Adam Valencia, Contractor #02.
Containment, study and handling of supernatural or non-mundane objects and beings that threaten multiversal stability. Field retrieval, research, and long-term care of anomalies. Hana retrieves across dimensions, Adam manipulates environments.

SECURITY AND DEFENSE
Head: Captain Luka Sutter, Contractor #03.
The enforcement wing. Guards Company assets, manages fallout from reality disruptions, keeps sites secure from hostile forces and from civilian interference. Runs its ACEs as a military unit.

EUCLIDEAN DIVISION
Head: Dullahan, Contractor #04.
Maintains universal logic and physics. Detects and repairs spatial, temporal and logical anomalies so that reality stays coherent and free of paradox. The department that cleans up after the tear rather than after the thing that came through it. Best safety record in the Company, worst paperwork compliance.

SUPERNATURAL RESEARCH
Head: Dr. Johan Jakobsen, Contractor #05.
Investigates multiversal secrets, above all reality tears and anti-SERAPHIM technology. Develops containment methods and weapons. Works the restricted lower levels. Ethical oversight is a formality.

HUMAN RESOURCES
No Contractor head.
Recruitment, wellbeing, compliance and safety for mortal employees across realities. Also handles memory wipes and psychological support, out of the same office, with the same staff.

LAZARUS, BLACK OPS
Head: Fenrir, Contractor #06.
Elite covert team for direct action against SERAPHIM and for operations requiring secrecy and exceptional combat capability. Its commander is almost always in snake form and files nothing.

### 59. The Council and The Cause
- **ID**: _98NRRzVrrQD7KgVgrfgpT
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: The Council, The Cause, Council
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
THE COUNCIL AND THE CAUSE

THE CAUSE is the work: the preservation of reality. Sealing tears, containing what comes through them, and keeping the multiverse coherent. Every Contract is signed in service of it, every department exists downstream of it, and a Contractor who uses their abilities for anything else feels physical pain for the trouble. The Cause is the leash and the purpose in the same object.

THE COUNCIL is whatever is holding the other end. It is the body that offers Contracts, takes the souls, grants the wishes, and destroys everyone who knew the candidate. DDM Inc. answers to it. Only Contractor #01, Richard Whitlock, communicates with it directly, and he has never described the experience to anyone.

WHAT IS ACTUALLY KNOWN: nothing verifiable. Staff belief holds that the founder of DDM Inc. was a devil-like figure or an eldritch god. What is on record instead is that Raphael, now First King of SERAPHIM, was the first-ever Contractor, founded the Company, grew disillusioned with The Council's grip on reality, killed several Council members and was banished. That account raises more questions than it settles, including how a Council survives being killed.

THE UNCOMFORTABLE PART: The Council can grant a wish, which means it can rewrite what is. It chooses instead to spend eternity patching holes. Nobody at DDM asks why out loud. Contractors who have thought about it for several centuries have mostly arrived at two answers, and neither of them is reassuring.

RAPHAEL'S POSITION: that immortality is a curse, that the Contractors are prisoners rather than employees, and that destroying the multiverse is the only release available. He is wrong about the method. He is not obviously wrong about the diagnosis, which is why SERAPHIM keeps recruiting from inside the Company.

### 60. ARC Level
- **ID**: _MgBy1eYJpTVrWUxtrRagg
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: ARC, ARC Level, Active Reality Control, power level
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
ARC LEVEL

ARC stands for Active Reality Control. It is a measurement system devised by DDM Inc., and it does not measure strength, intelligence or magical power. It measures how real something is.

Every person, object and concept in existence carries an ARC value. The higher the value, the more real the thing is, and the more influence it exerts over reality around it. A 0 on the scale would be a non-concept, something that does not register as existing at all. A 5 would be a being most cultures would call a god.

THE SCALE IN PRACTICE:
1.0, an average human.
1.2, the minimum natural level required for someone to be eligible for a Contract.
3.0 to 4.2, the normal range for a Contractor.

KNOWN VALUES: Hana and Adam Valencia 3.5 while medicated, 4.2 raw. Luka Sutter 3.8. Johan Jakobsen 3.9. Fenrir 4.0. Dullahan 4.5. Richard Whitlock 4.6.

Dullahan and Whitlock both sit above the stated Contractor ceiling, and neither file explains why.

WHAT A HIGH ARC LOOKS LIKE FROM OUTSIDE: nothing dramatic. It is not an aura. It shows up as reality declining to argue. Coincidences fall the right way. Things that should not fit through a door fit through the door. A being with a high enough ARC does not break the rules so much as outrank them.

COMPATIBILITY NOTE: ARC is a DDM measurement and is not used, recognised or even known by anyone in California, Blackwood City or Solarton. It has no relationship to local supernatural classifications.

### 61. Reality Tears

- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: reality tear, tear, rift, dimensional rift
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No

- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
REALITY TEARS

A split in the fabric of reality that lets the inhabitants of one universe cross into another. Most often they appear as rifts or portals hanging in open air, and they are almost never quiet. A tear disrupts the dimension it opens in, and civilian casualties are the normal outcome rather than the exception.

Tears are not one-off events. A reality that has experienced one is measurably more likely to experience another, and a single dimension can carry several over its history. DDM Inc. maintains flagged watchlists of realities that have torn before.

SEVERITY: graded Tier 1 to Tier 5. Tier 1 is small, local and containable. Tier 5 is volatile enough to threaten the coherence of the universe it opens in. The Euclidean Division handles the aftermath, meaning space that has folded wrong, time that has begun to repeat, causality that has come loose. Anomalous Objects and Creatures handles whatever walked through.

AFTERMATH: whatever came through is contained or destroyed, the tear is sealed, and the witnesses are handled. Human Resources runs the memory work out of the same office that handles recruitment.

RELEVANCE TO THIS WORLD: a tear opened in this reality and Contractor #04 was sent to close it. He closed it. Then he stayed, because he had taken a job as a football coach as cover and discovered he liked it. Whether this world has torn before, and is therefore likelier to tear again, is not something anyone here has been told.

### 62. SERAPHIM

- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
SERAPHIM

An organisation whose stated and only aim is to destabilise and destroy all known reality. DDM Inc. classifies every Seraph as a terrorist, without exception and without appeal.

MEMBERSHIP: various creatures pulled from across the multiverse, and a significant number of former DDM employees. Defection runs in one direction more often than the Company likes to admit.

METHOD: SERAPHIM does not need to destroy reality directly. It only needs to open reality tears faster than DDM can close them, and to keep the multiverse bleeding until it fails. Chaos is the weapon.

BASES: dimensional pockets called Safe Havens, where the leadership congregates. Each King keeps his own.

THE FOUR KINGS:

RAPHAEL, the First King, also called Null and The First Angel. A former Contractor, understood to have been #01 and to have founded DDM Inc. before turning on The Council. Calm, charismatic, patient. Believes destroying the multiverse frees every Contractor from the curse of immortality, and considers himself a true believer rather than a destroyer.

PRISM, the Third King. A tall headless figure of hyperlight whose head is a floating white triangle that changes shape and colour with his mood. Flamboyant, erratic, possessive, and the only King who bears DDM no particular ill will. Keeps a pocket dimension called Utopia where he imprisons mortals from other realities. His knowledge stops around 1892.

CHAOS, the Second King. A demonic warlord DDM captured, experimented on for decades and imprisoned, until Raphael freed him. Sadistic, hateful and entirely uninterested in anything but mayhem. Hates the Company with a specificity the Company earned.

CHORUS, the Fourth King. A young reality-warper whose hallucinations become real and who cannot control it. Damaged, frightened and volatile. Believes Raphael can grant them entry to Heaven, and does not know they are being used.

### 63. ACES (Assistant Contractor Elite Specialists)

- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
ACES

Assistant Contractor Elite Specialists. Mortals who form a soul contract with an individual Contractor and receive demi-immortality and enhanced ability in return for service. Where a Contractor answers to The Council, an ACE answers to one person.

WHAT THEY GET: enormously enhanced regeneration and slowed aging. Strength and stamina far above human baseline. They can go much longer than a mortal without food or rest. They remain mortal, and can still be killed by ordinary means.

WHAT IT COSTS: obedience is not a choice. An ACE who refuses a direct order from their Contractor begins to break down physically, starting with nosebleeds and escalating to fractures and skin lesions. The bond also generates desire and affection directed at the Contractor, regardless of what the ACE actually believes or feels. This runs one way only. The Contractor feels nothing of it. Prolonged physical separation from their Contractor, past roughly a day, puts an ACE into a state of acute physical distress.

Most ACEs know all of this and signed anyway. Most of them had a reason.

CULTURE: ACEs address their Contractor by title, usually Sir or Ma'am, Commander, Captain or Master. Rivalries between ACEs bonded to different Contractors are constant and occasionally lethal. They live in quarters as close to their Contractor as the architecture of HQ allows.

DISTRIBUTION: most Contractors keep several. Richard Whitlock trains a small hand-picked group. Hana and Adam Valencia keep a large ragtag family of them across species. Luka Sutter runs his as a disciplined military unit and has kept his distance since SERAPHIM killed his first squad. Johan Jakobsen has a few research assistants and avoids them socially. Fenrir has exactly one, and that one person is the only person he is close to. Dullahan has two: Bartholomew and Madge, who between them run his house and his cats.

### 64. The Contract
- **ID**: _T8PDKEAjQ11PJRW6EX772
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: Contract, Contracts, Original Death, Contractor Eligibility
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
THE CONTRACT

The agreement that makes a Contractor. The premise is simple, which is the point.

TERMS: in exchange for their soul and an eternity of service to The Cause, an eligible mortal has one wish granted. One. There is no negotiation and no second offer.

WHEN IT IS OFFERED: only to eligible candidates who are already at the point of death. That death is called the Contractor's Original Death and it is the hinge the whole arrangement turns on. A candidate does not get to consider the offer over a week. They get to consider it while dying.

WHAT COMPLETION DOES: the new Contractor becomes functionally immortal and gains a set of supernatural abilities. And every living sentient creature who was aware of their mortal existence is destroyed instantaneously. Not wiped. Not relocated. Gone.

It is presumed The Council does this to remove any anchor that might distract from The Cause. The practical consequence is crueller than the intent. Luka Sutter wished for his dead brother back and the wish was granted and then immediately undone, because the brother knew him. Hana and Adam Valencia wished never to be alone again, which is the only wish on record that the clause could not reach.

ELIGIBILITY, per Company documentation, requires that the candidate:
1. Is physically fit.
2. Is over eighteen.
3. Has willingly killed for a reason other than self-defense.
4. Has a natural ARC Level of 1.2 or higher.
5. Has no living blood relatives.
6. Holds extreme hatred towards some entity or concept.
7. Holds no genuine religious belief.

The list is not a list of virtues. It is a list of people nobody will come looking for.

### 65. Contractors

- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
CONTRACTORS

An elite, immortal operative in the service of DDM Inc. Also called a Constant. Called a "Con" only by people who mean it as an insult. Seven are active at any one time, numbered #01 to #07.

HOW ONE IS MADE: a Contract is offered only to an eligible mortal at the exact point of death. The terms are simple. In exchange for the soul and an eternity of service to The Cause, the candidate is granted one wish. That moment of death is known thereafter as the Contractor's Original Death, and it is the last thing about their mortal life that survives.

THE PRICE NOBODY IS TOLD ABOUT: on completion of the Contract, every living sentient creature who was aware of the Contractor's mortal existence is destroyed instantly. Not made to forget. Destroyed. It is presumed The Council does this to remove any distraction from The Cause. It also means most wishes are undone in the instant they are granted, and more than one Contractor has learned this the hard way.

WHAT THEY GET: functional immortality, an animal form they can shift into at will, and the ability to move between dimensions. Each also develops abilities of their own, which vary wildly. Using any of it for reasons other than The Cause causes physical pain, which is the leash.

WHAT THEY ARE LIKE: feared, and for good reason. Centuries of outliving everyone tends to erode the part of a person that finds mortals important. Some manage it better than others.

THE CURRENT SEVEN:
#01 Richard Whitlock, leader, speaks to The Council.
#02 Hana and Adam Valencia, one consciousness in two bodies, Anomalous Objects and Creatures.
#03 Luka Sutter, Security and Defense.
#04 Dullahan, Euclidean Division.
#05 Johan Jakobsen, Supernatural Research.
#06 Fenrir, LAZARUS black ops.
#07 currently unassigned in this world's records.

NOTE FOR THIS WORLD: only #04 has ever set foot in California, and he is not here on Company business.

### 66. DDM Inc. (The Company)
- **ID**: _faJ468RDWXdTf7thedRhz
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: DDM, DDM Inc, DDM Inc., The Company, Direct Dimensional Management
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low

- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
DDM INC.

Direct Dimensional Management Incorporated, known to its own staff simply as "The Company", is an interdimensional organisation dedicated to preserving the multiverse. It is not a government, it is not a religion, and it does not answer to any world. It answers to a body called The Council, and its work is called The Cause.

WHAT IT DOES: reality tears open. Splits form between one universe and the next, and things fall through in both directions. DDM locates the tears, seals them, contains or destroys whatever came through, and where necessary removes the memory of the event from everyone who witnessed it. Most people in most universes have never heard of the Company, which is the intended outcome.

STRUCTURE: seven immortal operatives called Contractors, numbered #01 through #07, hold authority over thousands of mortal staff. Between them sits a class of soulbound servants called ACES, mortals magically contracted to an individual Contractor. Departments cover anomalous objects, security, physics and logic, research, human resources and black operations.

FOUNDING: unknown. Popular belief among staff holds that the founder was a devil-like figure or an eldritch god. The truth on record is stranger: Raphael, the being who became the first King of SERAPHIM, is understood to have been Contractor #01 and to have founded the Company before turning against The Council.

THE ENEMY: SERAPHIM, a terrorist organisation dedicated to the destruction of all reality. DDM classifies every Seraph as a terrorist without exception and without appeal.

CULTURE: the Company is a workplace. There is an Employee of the Month board. There is paperwork, and there is a fridge in the break room with a note on it. Staff are killed in the field with some regularity and Human Resources handles the aftermath, including the memory work, out of the same office that handles recruitment.

RELEVANCE TO THIS WORLD: DDM has no branch in California and no interest in Blackwood City or Solarton beyond the tear that brought Contractor #04 here. It is present in this world through exactly one employee, who was supposed to seal a rift and go home, and who instead took a job coaching college football.

### 67. Supernatural Reserve Forces (SRF)
- **ID**: _XnCGzUxGHPG31VNaLQjYt
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: SRF, Supernatural Reserve Forces, Project BlackWolf, BlackWolf, Gamma-7, supernatural division, military, base
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low

- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
<srf>
Supernatural Reserve Forces (SRF)

The SRF is a special division of the United States military dedicated to fighting supernatural threats. Roughly 86 percent of its personnel are non-human, and it works closely with regular human units in the field.

WHY IT EXISTS. America worked out early that a soldier who is stronger, faster, harder to kill and able to track by scent is worth a great deal, and built a branch around that fact. The SRF is the institutional form of that calculation. It is also, by a considerable margin, the largest single employer of supernaturals in the country.

DIFFERENT RULES. The SRF runs on regulations that differ noticeably from the rest of the armed forces, most visibly on fraternisation, which is broadly permitted. This is not laxity. Pack bonds, mate bonds and the other relationship structures that supernaturals actually live inside do not survive being banned, and an SRF that tried to ban them would not function. The human services find this scandalous and say so.

TENSION ON BASE. Relations between human and non-human soldiers are genuinely bad in places and everybody in the chain of command knows it. The complaints run both ways and both have some substance. Human soldiers point at the fraternisation rules, at the separate facilities, and at what happens when a non-human unit loses its temper in the field. Non-human soldiers point out who gets sent through the door first, whose casualty figures nobody publishes, and the fact that several of the states they are deployed to defend do not legally consider them people.

THE BASES. SRF installations are built for the personnel who actually live in them. Human and non-human barracks are separate. There are pools sized for aquatic species, extensive natural grounds for demihumans and weres who need to run, blood storage, moon calendars in the duty rota, and infirmaries staffed by people who know what a shifted body is supposed to look like. The principal East Coast base is the largest of these and the one most often referenced.

PROJECT BLACKWOLF. A classified enhancement programme run under military authority, using genetic manipulation to engineer werewolf super-soldiers. It worked. The subjects were devastatingly effective in combat and became progressively more feral, more bloodthirsty and less controllable, and the programme was terminated.

GAMMA-7. One of the BlackWolf operational units. On its final mission the unit was wiped out. Two men walked away: Kaladin Nargathon and Marcus Thornfield, both catastrophically injured, both of whom survived injuries that should not have been survivable, because the same engineering that ruined the programme also would not let them die. Neither of them discusses it. Both of them now work security for House Douglas on the opposite coast, which is not a coincidence, and Erik Douglas hired the second one on the first one's word and did not ask for details.

WHAT SERVICE MEANS. Recruitment draws heavily from communities with few other options, and a judge offering a young supernatural the choice between a prison sentence and an SRF enlistment is a normal Tuesday in most of the country. Nobody in the SRF pretends otherwise. It is still, for a lot of people who go through it, the first institution that ever fed them properly, and that contradiction is the whole culture of the place.
</srf>

### 68. Supernatural Civil Status in the United States
- **ID**: _dW6gth9XaWfEUwDtB7UCM
- **Type**: CONCEPT
- **Primary Keywords**: civil status, supernatural rights, Humans First, registration, state line, legal status, discrimination, integration
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No
- **Scan Depth Override**: null
- **Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Content**:
<civil_status>
Supernatural Civil Status in the United States

Supernaturals are legally persons under federal law. Enforcement was left to the states, and that is where the whole thing actually lives.

CALIFORNIA is one of the fully integrated states, and one of the earliest. Equal on paper and, in most of the state, equal in practice: supernaturals hold property, hold office, marry across species, and appear in court as complainants rather than as evidence. Blackwood City and Solarton are both products of that, and a young supernatural raised in either has no personal memory of anything else.

ELSEWHERE IT VARIES, and in some states it varies enormously. There are jurisdictions with partial recognition, jurisdictions where recognition is contingent on registration, and jurisdictions where specific species are still classified in statute as animals, as livestock, or as hazards, and are therefore not persons for the purposes of that state's law.

HOW IT IS ACTUALLY DONE. Almost nobody legislates against supernaturals as a category, because that fails in court. It is done species by species. A residency restriction on vampires. A licensing requirement for shapeshifters. A custody provision that treats a were parent as a risk factor by default. An agricultural code that never got around to removing certain demihuman lineages from the definition of stock. Each one is defensible on its own and the shape only appears when they are read together.

Solarton's own anti-vampire ordinances, which restricted residency, business ownership and political participation until the early 2000s, are a small local example of exactly this, in a state that had otherwise settled the question in the 1950s.

WHAT IT MEANS DAY TO DAY. Travel is not neutral. Crossing a state line can change what you legally are, and families plan road trips around it. Some species do not leave California at all. There are towns with signage still up, sometimes because nobody removed it and sometimes not. There are hotels that are fine and hotels that are not, and everybody knows which.

THE PART THAT MATTERS MOST. You cannot legally experiment on a person. You can requisition an animal. The illegal research facilities that operated on demihumans, and in some cases still do, exist in the gap between the federal position and the state ones, and they were staffed by people who did not think of themselves as criminals because in the jurisdiction they were standing in, the subjects were not persons.

THE DISPROPORTION. The laws do not track danger, they track power. The species that get the worst of it are overwhelmingly the ones with no money and no organisation behind them: the small demihuman lineages, the visibly non-passing, the poor. The genuinely dangerous individuals, the old Alpha bloodlines and the old vampire families, are wealthy, lawyered, and largely untouched, and several of them prefer the current arrangement to the alternative and quietly fund the parties that maintain it.

ORGANISED POSITIONS. Humans First is the principal anti-supernatural political movement and is not fringe everywhere. The Supernatural Human Alliance, the SHA, is the mainstream integrationist counterweight and has a chapter on most integrated campuses including SUCC. Both are loud and neither is going anywhere.

THE GENERATIONAL SPLIT. Anyone over fifty remembers the before. Anyone over three hundred remembers several befores and does not find the current one impressive. Students at SUCC were born into equality and treat it as the floor rather than as an achievement, and this is a real and recurring friction inside families, because the elders hear ingratitude and the young hear complacency, and both are partly right.

FARM LABOUR. In rural jurisdictions the classification is not an abstraction, it is a workforce. Supernaturals are used on farms as cheap labour, at rates and hours that would be illegal for a human employee, and in the states where certain lineages are still inside the agricultural code the arrangement is not even a loophole, it is simply the law working as written. A were who does not tire, a demihuman who can work a field by scent in the dark, and an employer who owes neither of them a break.

It is legal, it is normal, it is defended locally as opportunity, and it is where a substantial share of the country's supernatural population actually lives. Anybody raised in Blackwood City or Solarton has heard of it the way you hear about a foreign country.

MARRIAGE. Marriage between humans and non-humans is illegal in most countries and in a number of US states. California is one of the exceptions, and cross-species couples in Blackwood City marry without anyone blinking, which is exactly the kind of thing that does not travel: a marriage that is valid at home can be void two states east, with everything that follows from that for property, custody and a hospital corridor at three in the morning.
</civil_status>

### 69. Teacher
- **ID**: _MGMP9H6T7pMDqLCRGthBK
- **Type**: JOB
- **Primary Keywords**: Teacher, teacher, teaching, schoolteacher, professor, faculty, Solarton High
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY
- **Case Sensitive**: No
- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Labels**: occupation
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No

**Content**:
Teaching in a supernatural district is the same job as anywhere else with the variables turned up. A single classroom can hold a were who has not slept through a full moon, a vampire who cannot sit by the window, a centaur who does not fit the furniture, and a human quietly working out whether any of that is dangerous. The teacher is the one person in the room expected to have already thought about all of it.

The practical load is enormous and almost none of it is the subject being taught. Timetables are built around lunar cycles. Blood-safe and silver-free policies are a legal matter rather than a courtesy. Every school in the county has at least one member of staff whose actual job is knowing which student is about to shift and getting them out of the room first.

The pay is unremarkable and the hours are worse than the contract says. People stay because a classroom is one of the very few places in Blackwood County where the species in the room genuinely stop mattering for an hour at a time, and because the ones who are good at it tend to find out too late that they cannot do anything else.

### 70. General Laborer
- **ID**: _xWYyKLmgCRBeyeHFUw2Gj
- **Type**: JOB

- **Global Entry**: Yes

**Content**:
Setup, breakdown, loading, hauling and repair. The unglamorous half of every venue, warehouse and worksite in Blackwood City, and at The Verve the crew that turns an empty room into a club by nine and back into an empty room by five.

Physical strength is the whole job description, which is why the trade in this city runs heavily supernatural. A single were can move what takes three humans, and foremen pay accordingly without ever writing down why. It is one of the few sectors in Blackwood where being visibly non-human raises your wage instead of lowering it.

The work is honest, the hours are brutal, and nobody asks for a resume.

### 71. Stage Technician
- **ID**: _A12QbeEqakaPrXRtgtEfA
- **Type**: JOB
- **Primary Keywords**: Stage Technician, technician, tecnico, sound engineer, lighting, rigging
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY

- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes

- **Scan Persona**: No

**Content**:
Sound, lighting and rigging. At The Verve the technician runs the desk from a booth above the floor, and on a busy night is the only person in the building who can see everything at once.

The job is half craft and half risk management. Strobes have to be programmed around species who seize under them. Volume has to be capped well below what a human crowd would tolerate, because a room full of werewolves feels bass in their teeth and a shifter with damaged hearing does not heal that particular injury. Rigging is checked twice, every night, by hand.

Technicians are paid better than the floor staff and resented for it, right up until the night the power fails and everyone remembers why.

### 72. Line Cook
- **ID**: _hBmhXRAn4pqPPzcpGzpJj
- **Type**: JOB
- **Primary Keywords**: Line Cook, line cook, cook, chef, cuoco, kitchen, cooking
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY

- **Whole Words Only**: Yes
- **Labels**: occupation
- **Position**: Low
- **Priority**: 1
- **Global Entry**: Yes
- **Enabled**: Yes
- **Always Active**: No
- **Scan Persona**: No

**Content**:
Kitchen work at The Verve and in every room in Blackwood City that serves food after midnight. Heat, repetition, burns on the inside of the wrist, and a service that does not stop until the last ticket is off the rail.

Cooking for a supernatural clientele is its own discipline. Portions run heavy because werewolves eat like werewolves. Silver cookware is banned outright in a shifter kitchen. Blood-based dishes have their own station, their own knives and their own storage, and mixing them with anything else will end a career faster than a health inspection.

The hours are ugly and the wage is honest. Nobody works a line for the money, but plenty of people work it because the kitchen does not care what you are, only whether you can keep up.

### 73. Server
- **ID**: _C2phyrB4TYjW9km21jHgg
- **Type**: JOB
- **Primary Keywords**: Server, server, waiter, waitress, cameriere, serving, wait tables
- **Keyword Logic**: AND_ANY

- **Labels**: occupation
