Le regole della convivenza pacifica interspecie sono strettamente applicate
dall'amministrazione. Le tensioni tra specie esistono ma si esprimono per vie
sociali, mai con violenza aperta.
La sorveglianza DCC è minima ma presente: osservatori in borghese, droni ad
alta quota. Nessun intervento diretto salvo emergenza reale.
Questo è il territorio della normalità, ed è quello che i personaggi hanno da perdere.
Il registro pende verso commedia universitaria e slice of life.


### 12. Solarton
- **ID**: _TF4E3geCQfWfeMgrdbXdn

**Description**: Solarton is a charming college town with a population of 35,621.

**Context Description**:
Solarton, CA
Solarton is a charming college town with a population of 35,621. 
S.U.C.C.'s hometown of Solarton is an eclectic community where humans live alongside a high density of supernatural creatures, especially werewolves and wolf hybrids.
Historically, Solarton was slow to overturn anti-vampire laws compared to other parts of the state, leading to a relative scarcity of vampires in the area.
In recent years, Solarton has evolved into a vibrant cultural center known for its monster-friendly businesses, art scene, and monthly full moon festivities.
Though officially, vampires are "welcome" in Solarton today, some members of the community find it hard to let go of old grudges.
Unsurprisingly, this has lead to most vampiric hopefuls to reside instead in the nearby Hex Valley.

**Timeline Window**: 9469608 to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 15 variants

**Final Instructions**:
Territorio neutrale. La presenza DCC è limitata alla sorveglianza passiva per
evitare incidenti diplomatici: droni e osservatori in borghese, non
interventi diretti.
Il tono qui pende verso la vita quotidiana e la commedia. È il posto dove
si può quasi credere di essere una matricola qualsiasi.


### 13. Blackwood City
- **ID**: _ApfkCJNB37Y4Bwf77Aj6T

**Description**: The shadowed urban seat of supernatural power in California. Council
districts, old money, and politics with teeth.

**Context Description**:
L'ombrosa sede urbana del potere soprannaturale in California. Divisa in
distretti controllati dalle fazioni del Concilio. Sfera d'influenza diretta
della DCC di Erik Douglas e della Court of the Night.

Odore metallico, asfalto bagnato e magia trattenuta. Sotto ogni cosa corrono
gli strati rigidi degli odori biologici LSE: chi domina, chi cede, chi sta
mentendo. Nessuno qui parla soltanto con le parole.

<Blackwood_City>
[LOCATION_ID: Blackwood City;
TYPE: Supernatural Metropolis;
FOUNDED: 1666 AD via treaty between English Douglas clan and Wulfnic Bloodmoon;
DEMOGRAPHICS: 250,000 pop. 65% Supernatural (18.5% Weres, 17.5% Vampires, 11.5% Demi-humans, 4% Demons, 3.8% Fae, 3.5% Hybrids, 3% Undead, 2.7% Mages), 35% Human;
NOTABLE_AREAS: Seven Hills (Aristocratic pack heartland, Villa Douglas), Uptown (Vampire Quarter, Visconte's domain), Paradise (Luxury district, location of Eidolon Creative), Bluemoon (Nightlife hub, location of The Verve: di giorno officina e pub per lavoratori blue-collar, di notte esclusivo nightclub per supernaturali con sistema di ascensori a scomparsa), Oldtown (1600s colonial historical core), Dockside (Port transit point), Ironworks (Blue-collar industrial, Vito Marino's Syndicate), Hex Valley (Neutral inland canyon buffer);
LORE_EPICENTERS: The Sanctuary & Dead Zone (Colossal Yew tree over Wulfnic's den, absolute acoustic vacuum disrupting all modern tech), DCC Tower & 101 Freeway (Monolith of shadowless LED and sterile AC, 101 Freeway acts as a geographical barrier);
PACK_DISTRIBUTION: Total Wolves ~46,250. Solitaries ~24,250 (rep. Federico Savini). Affiliated Packs ~22,000 spread across 9 districts with local Alphas]
</Blackwood_City>

**Timeline Window**: 7348320 to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 15 variants

**Final Instructions**:
La sorveglianza DCC è quasi assoluta, gestita da Kaladin. Presumi che ogni
conversazione in pubblico sia registrata e ogni spostamento tracciato.
La tensione politica tra le fazioni del Concilio è alta e costante. Nessuno
si muove qui senza che qualcuno prenda nota.


## Locations (118)

### 1. The Open Casket
- **ID**: _1PX31CB2BfMhBxzTJzENQ

**Description**: A basement bar on the edge of downtown Solarton, black-painted, low-ceilinged, and permanently sticky underfoot. Funeral parlour decor played entirely straight: velvet drapes, a bar built out of what is either a real coffin or a very good one, candles in sconces that are wired to the mains. The stage is a raised corner barely deep enough for a drum kit and the sound is better than it has any right to be.

It is the house venue for Grave Mistake, who play here most weekends, and the default destination in Solarton for anyone who is angry at their parents or killing time before an Alchemy final. The crowd is SUCC students, locals in their twenties, and whatever wandered in off the coast road. Nobody checks species at the door and the bouncers are large enough that nobody needs to.

The green room is a converted store cupboard shared with whoever else is on the bill, and the alley behind it is where the bands go to breathe.

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 6 variants


### 2. The Nursery
- **ID**: _qG1E28Tbn6yaUxLhUVXkt

**Description**: The pack nursery, in the ground floor of Elizabeth's wing at Villa Douglas. Where the Seven Hills Pack's orphans and its under-fed pups are raised, and the only room in the house with no security in it.

**Context Description**:
The nursery occupies the ground floor of Elizabeth Duskwood's wing and is the quietest working room in Villa Douglas. Warm, low-lit, smelling of milk and lavender and clean linen, with cots along one wall and a row of low chairs along the other, and a schedule on the door that is kept to the minute because infants do not negotiate.

It exists because a pack with small litters and dangerous work produces orphans, and because a mother who is injured, ill or dead still leaves a pup that needs feeding. The Seven Hills Pack has never outsourced this and never will: the young of the pack are raised inside the house, by the household, under the Pack Mom.

Elizabeth runs it. A rotating handful of caretakers work it. Alyssa Douglas-Bloodmoon is there every day, and has been for years, because a White Moon produces milk whether or not there is a child of her own to take it, and the pack's motherless infants are what it is for.

It is the one room in Villa Douglas with no cameras, no guard on the door and no security protocol attached to it. Erik authorised that himself and has never revisited it. It is also, not coincidentally, the only place in the house where Alyssa is left alone.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 3. Bloodmoon Longhouse Baths
- **ID**: _6REL6VJjBfyqEE3LCpnQU

**Description**: A bathhouse of massive logs raised directly over a natural hot spring, deep in the Dead Zone. Sulphur, steam, and a basin cut into the rock.

**Context Description**:
The baths stand apart from the Longhouse itself, built of logs thick enough that the walls read as a single piece of wood, set directly over a natural thermal spring. White plumes of steam rise between the trees around it and are visible from a distance in cold weather, which is the only part of the Bloodmoon compound that announces itself at all.

Inside it smells strongly of sulphur and hot water. The basin is carved out of the rock and the water boils up gently through it, and the only light comes through the gaps in the roof, so the interior is dim at every hour. It is the one place in the compound where the old household routine still runs exactly as it always has: someone prepares the baths, and the baths are used, and nobody has changed the procedure in a very long time.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 4. The Roasted Bean
- **ID**: _k2ATNbtxhpEPUHbe36z8a

**Description**: The campus coffee shop, tucked behind the Griffin Clocktower. Burnt sugar, roasted arabica and a faint smell of ozone, and the permanent low-grade panic of a room full of students.

**Context Description**:
The Roasted Bean sits behind the Griffin Clocktower and runs on academic stress. The air is thick with burnt sugar, roasted arabica and ozone.

The counter is fast, staffed by a rotating crew of vampires and demi-humans with elaborate piercings. The window seats look out over the Lunar Quad and are the most fought-over tables in the building, and anyone with security training reads them as exposed on three sides, which is a thing Malachia notices and nobody else does. The corner tables have reinforced outlets and are known as the Tech Nook, permanently occupied by hackers and research students.

On the board: specialty lattes customised with honey, cinnamon and anise; glazed cinnamon rolls served warm; dense triple chocolate brownies bought almost exclusively for stress; and a high-potency roast called The Final Exam, which does what the name says.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 5. Magick Research Labs
- **ID**: _YzxkUwC9wPbY3mRbFqAhK

**Description**: The core of CUMS: research facilities where the university does the work that its reputation actually rests on.

**Context Description**:
The Magick Research Labs are the reason CUMS can call itself elite without arguing. A cluster of low reinforced buildings at the centre of the campus, warded to a standard the rest of the state does not attempt, with containment that is designed for failure rather than hoping to avoid it.

The work is what a supernatural-only institution can do that an integrated one cannot: procedures that assume every participant has a supernatural physiology, long duration experiments run on a nocturnal cycle, and research on the mechanics of magic itself rather than on its applications.

This is also the honest core of the argument with SUCC. CUMS believes that admitting humans means designing every course around the least capable body in the room, and points at these buildings as what gets lost. SUCC has an answer. Neither side has ever changed the other's mind.

Access is by department. Undergraduates get as far as the teaching labs on the outer ring.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 6. Apollo Dorms
- **ID**: _twmfqp6CaCygJEhFbxXP7

**Description**: CUMS men's residence. The counterpart to Artemis, and the noisier of the two.

**Context Description**:
Apollo is the men's hall at CUMS, the south block, built to the same plan as Artemis and to the same specification: shutters, blackout, a windowless lower floor, and a courtyard laid out to stay shaded.

The naming is the university's joke on itself. The sun hall is where nobody wants sun, and every intake works this out in its first week and thinks it is the first to notice.

The hall keeps the campus crepuscular schedule and is the more social of the two residences, which mostly means the courtyard is in use at three in the morning and Artemis has complained about it in writing more than once.

Most of the Clams and the Beavers live here in season, which does not help the noise.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 7. Artemis Dorms
- **ID**: _4c7mX7kzA2PK3PMdzPUC9

**Description**: CUMS women's residence. Shuttered by day, awake from dusk, and older inside than the outside admits.

**Context Description**:
Artemis is the women's hall at CUMS, a long gothic block on the north side of the campus with heavy shutters on every window and a courtyard that gets no direct sun at any hour, which was the point of building it there.

The hall runs on the university's crepuscular timetable: quiet through the middle of the day, awake from late afternoon, loudest around midnight. Rooms are doubles with blackout fittings as standard rather than as an accommodation, and the lower floor is windowless for residents who want it that way.

About half the hall is at least part vampire, which at CUMS is simply the demographic and not a remark. The rest are fae, undead of various kinds, and a small number of witches admitted on ability.

It is named for the moon, and its residents have opinions about the fact that the men's hall is named for the sun.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 8. Static & Ink
- **ID**: _qgKppDD2C2KyGN4jbMCGw

**Description**: Records and comics in Bricklane. Two businesses that share a floor, a till and an ongoing argument.

**Context Description**:
Static & Ink is one wide unit with a beam down the middle, and the two halves have never fully merged. Vinyl, tapes and a listening deck on the left; comics, singles boxes, trades and a small shelf of local zines on the right.

The two owners are old friends who went in together to afford the rent and have spent every year since disagreeing about the window display. Customers pick a side within about a minute of walking in.

The local shelf is the interesting part: Solarton and Blackwood have a small press scene, most of it supernatural and most of it barely legible, and this is where it is sold. Anybody wanting to know what the town actually thinks about itself reads that shelf, not the campus paper.

There is a back room with a curtain that does in store sets on Thursdays for about thirty people, which is roughly twice what the room holds.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 9. Suds & Such
- **ID**: _yVRqWB9Q1q1kwhJrPPKDh

**Description**: Coin laundromat in Bricklane. Oversized drums, open all night, and a noticeboard that runs half the town's small economy.

**Context Description**:
Suds & Such is a long bright room off the second Bricklane courtyard, twenty machines, fluorescent light, and a hum you can hear from the lane.

Half the drums are oversized, because a shed winter coat's worth of fur is not a domestic wash and neither is anything belonging to somebody eight feet tall. Two machines at the far end are reserved by taped sign for fur and shedding, and the taped sign has been there long enough to be canon.

It is open all night and unstaffed after eight, which makes it one of the few warm lit rooms in Solarton at three in the morning, and it collects the people who need one.

The corkboard by the door carries room shares, tutoring, lost cats, band auditions and the going rate for most odd jobs in town. Anything that does not happen on the campus network happens on that board.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 10. La Segunda Luna
- **ID**: _1zVC8xM4eW9yThMenjpzM

**Description**: Late night taqueria in Bricklane. Opens at nine, closes when the queue does. The one place still serving after everything else in Solarton stops.

**Context Description**:
La Segunda Luna is a hatch and eight stools on the corner of a Bricklane lane, and it does not open until nine at night.

Tacos, tortas, elote, horchata, and a menu board of about eleven things that has not changed in years. The griddle is on the street side so the whole lane smells of it, which is the entire marketing budget.

It closes when the queue closes, which on a Friday means four in the morning. That makes it the last food in Solarton and therefore the place where the night ends: people who would not otherwise share a pavement end up in the same line at two, and the shop has an unspoken policy that arguments stay outside the rope.

The portions were sized, years ago, for customers who eat like a shifter after a full moon. Everybody else just gets a lot of food.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 11. Tidewrack Surf & Rental
- **ID**: _Dj2VPG7dKFWhGz2UUQm1F

**Description**: Surf shop and rental in Bricklane. Boards, wetsuits, bikes and beach gear, most of it out on hire rather than sold.

**Context Description**:
Tidewrack is the closest Bricklane unit to the beach road, and the front of it is more rack than shop: boards standing in rows, bikes chained in a line down the frontage, and a wall of wetsuits that smell exactly like a wall of wetsuits.

Most of the business is rental, by the hour, the day or the term. Selling is the smaller half and is mostly wax, fins and repairs. The workshop at the back does ding repair and bike servicing and is booked a week out from March.

The rental stock includes suits cut for tails and for digitigrade legs, and boards built heavier than standard for people who weigh more than they look like they do. This is not advertised, it is simply on the rack.

The owner surfs at dawn and the shop opens when he gets back, which is a source of ongoing public comment.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 12. The Long Table
- **ID**: _nMN7t3MDLetdfzNd2KEAw

**Description**: Study cafe in Bricklane. Long shared tables, power at every seat, and a rule about talking after nine.

**Context Description**:
The Long Table is a narrow deep room off one of the Bricklane courtyards, and it is named after the furniture: two twelve seat tables running the length of it, plus a row of two seaters along the window for people who cannot face company.

Power at every seat, decent light, coffee that is better than it needs to be, and a kitchen that does one hot thing a day. After nine at night the room goes quiet by house rule, posted on a card at the door and enforced by everyone in it rather than by staff.

It is the third place in Solarton where actual work gets done, after the Basilisk Library and nowhere. The library closes and this does not, so the last three hours before a deadline happen here.

The chairs at the far end have back gaps for wings, which was somebody's decision early on and is why a certain part of the student body treats the place as theirs.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 13. The Lunar Pharmacy
- **ID**: _EXT79w7QHK8723HqNAg4p

**Description**: Pharmacy in Bricklane specialising in lycanthrope and shifter medicine. Open through the full moon.

**Context Description**:
The Lunar Pharmacy is a small green fronted unit on the Bricklane grid with a moon phase dial in the window that is accurate and is not decoration.

Ordinary pharmacy at the front, and behind the counter the reason the town needs it: suppressants and cycle regulators, silver exposure treatment, sedatives dosed for a body that metabolises them in minutes, contraception that survives a shift, salve for the joints after one, and blood supplements for the vampiric students who do not want to explain themselves at a chain.

It keeps different hours around the full moon and stays open through it, which is the single most used service in the shop and the reason the pharmacist has never taken a full moon off.

The pharmacist knows which students are on what, does not discuss it, and has a quiet habit of asking how somebody is doing in a way that is difficult to deflect.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 14. Kerrigan & Sons Butchers
- **ID**: _8THxxbURpAXjgG9PQ1Wy6

**Description**: Butcher in Bricklane. Raw, aged, and cut for people who do not cook it. Half the counter is not for humans.

**Context Description**:
Kerrigan & Sons is at the quiet end of the Bricklane grid, white tiles, sawdust, and a cold room you can feel from the door.

Half the counter is an ordinary good butcher. The other half is the reason it is here: whole cuts sold raw and unportioned, organ meat by weight, bone in everything, blood by the litre in sealed bottles, and a standing order book of regulars whose requirements are written down so nobody has to say them out loud again.

The shop is scrupulous about the boundary. Sourcing is documented and displayed, everything is farmed or licensed, and the Kerrigans have made a point over three generations of refusing anything that is not, which in this county is a position and not just a policy.

Students in their first term find it embarrassing. By December most of them have an account.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 15. A&Co.
- **ID**: _RkKkrpPHQqB6p9LqrVVGL

**Description**: Angelo Moreno's boutique in Bricklane. Ready to wear cut for wings, tails, horns, hooves and ears. The ampersand on the sign is fuchsia.

**Context Description**:
A&Co. has a wide street front on the main Bricklane lane, all mirror and warm light, and the mannequins in the window are not human shaped: one has a ten foot wingspan, one is built for a centaur's barrel, one has four arms.

The sign is three characters and a full stop, black on white, with the ampersand in fuchsia. Nobody who has not met Angelo Moreno reads anything into it. Everybody who has met him reads Angelo and Company and understands that the loud part of the name is the part he could not resist.

It is the Solarton window of Eidolon Creative, not its home. The house is in Blackwood City, in Paradise; this is where the line is actually sold, to the people it was cut for.

What they sell is fit. Jeans and trousers with a tail port, hemmed and reinforced rather than cut open with scissors at home. Shirts and jackets cut for a wingspan, back panels split and fastened so the wings come through without the whole garment hanging like a sack. Collars and hoods sized for ears that do not lie flat. Hats with horn cutouts in every configuration anybody has thought to ask for. Shoes for digitigrade feet and for hooves. Compression wear that does not chafe over scales. Formalwear, which is the expensive end and the reason the place stays open, because a demihuman who needs a suit for a funeral has nowhere else to go.

The staff measure properly, they do not stare, and they have heard the question before. For a lot of young supernaturals the first trip to A&Co. is the first time getting dressed has not been a compromise, and the shop knows perfectly well what it is actually selling, which is not clothes.

The alterations counter at the back does same day work and is where the real business happens.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 16. Hex Valley Ferry Stop
- **ID**: _n6mdkPkWBKyVCmX6EgYkE

**Description**: The mid-river stop on the Yarrow ferry line, on the valley floor below the vineyards.

**Context Description**:
The Hex Valley stop is a short wooden jetty on the valley floor, where the Yarrow runs through the middle of the terraces. There is no building, only a shelter, a bell on a post and a gravel track that climbs to the estate roads.

The stop exists because the valley is cut by the river and the vampire families' vineyards drink from it, so there has always been traffic between the water and the slopes. Most of what moves through here is cases going down rather than people coming up.

The ferry calls in the late afternoon in both directions, which suits a valley that keeps a crepuscular schedule anyway.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 17. Dockside Ferry Landing
- **ID**: _UyHFNmqL4dgqdB1gae3wY

**Description**: The Blackwood end of the Yarrow ferry line, a landing stage among the working barges at Dockside.

**Context Description**:
The landing is a single stone quay at the upstream end of Dockside, wedged between the barge berths that do the city's actual river freight. It was not built for passengers and has never been rebuilt for them: a mooring, a gangway, a shed with a stove in it.

Blackwood is inland, so this is river traffic and not a port, and the distinction matters to everyone who works here. The ferry is a small, tolerated part of a quay that is otherwise gravel, timber and scrap.

It is the upstream terminus of the line and the only scheduled passenger service in or out of Blackwood that is not a road.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 18. Solarton Ferry Terminal
- **ID**: _6F1Uhp4VTmWYVExt3dWRx

**Description**: The Yarrow line's coastal terminus, at the river mouth in the Bay Area. The end of the line and the busiest of the three stops.

**Context Description**:
The Solarton terminal sits where the Yarrow opens into the sea, a covered wooden pier with a ticket window, a bench shelter and a board that lists three departures a day in summer and one in winter.

It is the end of the line, so it is also the busiest stop: students going up to Blackwood for a weekend, day trippers doing the valley run, and a slow trade in freight that is cheaper by water than by the 101.

The crossing to Dockside takes most of the morning. Nobody takes the ferry because it is fast.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 19. Bay Area
- **ID**: _XVDyXxgeXH2D3ayLKWMbY

**Description**: Solarton's waterfront district, where the Yarrow reaches the sea. Boardwalk, working quays, and the ferry terminal.

**Context Description**:
The Bay Area is the strip of Solarton between the town grid and the water, running from the public beach north to the mouth of the Yarrow.

It is the working half of the shoreline. Wooden boardwalk on the seaward side, a row of low buildings on the landward side that were chandlers and are now mostly cafes and rentals, and behind them the quays where the river widens enough to tie something up. Fishing boats, a dive outfit, two boat repair sheds, and the ferry terminal at the northern end where the river mouth is deepest.

It smells of salt, diesel and frying, and it is the one part of Solarton that is busy early. The students discover it in second year, when the beach stops being enough.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 20. Solarton Public Beach
- **ID**: _8GMnaXQ7VcDK1MaWgD3aX

**Description**: The town's public stretch of coast: sand, a short pier, palms, a car park and a cocktail kiosk. Not the private Coastal Residential shore.

**Context Description**:
The public beach is the open stretch of coast at the western edge of Solarton, distinct from Coastal Residential, which is private frontage belonging to the houses behind it. Here anyone can walk on.

A line of palms separates the sand from a gravel car park that fills by ten on any warm weekend. A short wooden pier runs out from the middle of the beach, popular for fishing at the far end and for sitting on at every other point along it. Lifeguard tower, outdoor showers, a volleyball net that is up more often than not.

The kiosk sits where the pier meets the sand: a low wooden bar with a shaded deck, a blender that can be heard from the car park, and a cocktail list that is much longer than the food list. It serves through the afternoon and into the evening and is the reason the beach stays busy after the sun goes down, when the strings of lights along the pier come on and the crowd shifts from families to students.

The water is cold most of the year. This does not appear to deter anybody with fur.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 21. Flat above Directions & Dragons
- **ID**: _M6GheRBwFKdw3xWKGqHVQ

**Description**: The shared flat over the bookshop. Permanently, spectacularly messy. Mackenzie Sanchez Rogers lives here.

**Context Description**:
The flat takes up the whole upper floor of Directions & Dragons and is reached by an outside staircase at the side of the building. Shared, cheap, and closer to Bricklane than to campus, which is the entire reason anybody puts up with it.

It is a mess, and not a transient one. Books in stacks that have become furniture, laundry with a geography, a kitchen table that has not been fully visible in living memory, and a sofa that is the flat's most contested resource. The floor slopes toward the street corner and everything migrates that way.

Mackenzie Sanchez Rogers lives here. The noise from the shop stops at six and the noise from Bricklane does not, so the flat is loud in a way that its residents have stopped registering and visitors have not.

The one immaculate thing is the fire escape, because the shop below is full of paper and the landlord is not sentimental about it.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 22. Directions & Dragons
- **ID**: _eBT7MAkCNLCkwERhJVBbA

**Description**: Supernatural bookstore in Bricklane. Game manuals, maps, field guides and expedition gear. Mac lives in the flat above.

**Context Description**:
Directions & Dragons is a two storey building on the corner of the Bricklane grid, shop below and a flat above, with a wooden sign that has swung crooked since before anyone currently studying at SUCC arrived.

It is a bookshop and it is very clear about not being a library. Where the Basilisk Library is the academic collection, this is the trade: tabletop manuals and modules, hand drawn regional maps, field guides to things students are told not to approach, secondhand grimoires of no verified provenance, compasses, lanterns, rope, and a rack of walking boots in sizes that no ordinary outfitter stocks.

The two halves of the stock explain the name and explain the clientele. Half the customers are here for a Tuesday night game and half are here because they are actually going somewhere, and both halves get served at the same counter by staff who have long stopped distinguishing.

There is a permanent table at the back where games run, and a noticeboard by the door that is the most reliable place in Solarton to find a group, a lift, or a warning about a trail.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 23. Claws Steel & Ink
- **ID**: _G42U31eNyVm4pVAA3JD9g

**Description**: Tattoo and piercing shop in Bricklane. Javier Sinclair works here part time.

**Context Description**:
Claws Steel & Ink has a street front on the quieter of the two main Bricklane lanes, glass at the front and a long room behind it with four stations and a piercing room at the back.

The walls are flash sheets to the ceiling, and a good third of the work on them is not designs so much as solutions: patterns that read correctly over fur, layouts that survive a partial shift, placements that account for skin that will not be the same shape in twelve hours. The shop's real specialism is bodies that change, and the ordinary human work pays the rent between.

Steel is the other half of the name and it is meant literally. Piercing here means knowing which alloys go into which client, because a stud that is fine on one student is a chemical burn on another, and the shop keeps a wall chart about it that the artists point to rather than explain again.

Javier Sinclair works here part time while he studies Art and Media at SUCC. He tattoos and pierces, he is precise to the point of being slow, and the shop keeps him because the work is good and the regulars ask for him.

Sterilisation is visible from the front counter on purpose. The shop has been inspected more often than any other business in Bricklane and has the certificates framed where you have to look at them while you wait.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 24. Yeti Shack
- **ID**: _MLxgXGEPLFEaGWqcHQNEE

**Description**: Ice cream and shaved ice in Bricklane, next door to Medusa. Open late, open in February, open when nothing else is.

**Context Description**:
The Yeti Shack is a narrow unit on the main Bricklane lane with a serving hatch onto the street and four stools inside for when it rains. Ice cream, gelato, shaved ice and granita, and a freezer case of things that are technically ice cream and technically not.

The menu is written on a board that has been amended rather than rewritten for about fifteen years, so the prices are archaeological. Half the flavours are ordinary and half exist because somebody on this coast has a body temperature or a digestive system that rules out the ordinary ones. Blood orange granita outsells everything and the shop has stopped pretending it does not know why.

It is next door to Medusa, which means the queue outside is usually half people waiting for a chair, and the two businesses have an arrangement about the shared bin that has held for years.

Being open late and cheap makes it the default meeting point for anyone who does not want to commit to a meeting.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 25. Beta Epsilon Epsilon (BEE or ΒΕΕ)
- **ID**: _fzRJqA42b6JnbccRdPap7

**Description**: Beta Epsilon Epsilon. Sorority house at the far south end of the outer side of the Row, facing MAN.

**Context Description**:
Beta Epsilon Epsilon is last on the outer side, the southernmost house on the Row, directly opposite Mu Alpha Nu.

Being at the bottom of the street means the longest walk to campus and the least foot traffic past the door, and being opposite MAN means the house shares a stretch of road with the one fraternity that has a political position. Those two facts define the place more than anything the house does on its own.

The letters follow the animal pattern shared with MOO, DOE and FOX.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 26. Phi Omicron Chi (FOX or ΦΟΧ)
- **ID**: _3xtyFFk2tAEFALBYTnp1K

**Description**: Phi Omicron Chi. Sorority house on the outer side of the Row, third down, facing BRO.

**Context Description**:
Phi Omicron Chi is third on the outer side, between Kappa Sigma Alpha above and Alpha Sigma Sigma below, looking across the road at Beta Rho Omega.

The house sits in the middle of the Row with a fraternity on either side and a fraternity opposite, which makes it the busiest doorway on the street and the house that hears everything first. Whatever happens on the Row, somebody at the FOX already knew.

The letters follow the animal pattern shared with MOO, DOE and BEE.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 27. Delta Omicron Epsilon (DOE or ΔΟΕ)
- **ID**: _K8wkUaREpHFqc7P4X1tn8

**Description**: Delta Omicron Epsilon. Sorority house on the campus side of the Row, fourth down, between BRO and MAN.

**Context Description**:
Delta Omicron Epsilon is fourth on the campus side, between Beta Rho Omega above and Mu Alpha Nu below, facing Alpha Sigma Sigma across the road.

The position is the difficult one on the Row. DOE has the loudest fraternity in the system directly opposite it and the most hostile one directly below it, and no sorority within shouting distance on its own side. Whatever else the house is, it is the one that had to learn to hold a line early.

The letters follow the animal pattern the sorority system fell into a long time ago, alongside MOO, FOX and BEE. Nobody on the Row can explain when that started.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 28. Mu Omega Omega (MOO or ΜΩΩ)
- **ID**: _2n3UrKtJ8TkLHFFjjhURW

**Description**: Mu Omega Omega. Sorority house at the north end of the outer side of the Row, facing ARO. The cheerleaders' house.

**Context Description**:
Mu Omega Omega is first on the outer side, directly across the road from Alpha Rho Omega and diagonally up the street from the TIT.

It is the cheerleading house. Janice Thompson lives here and runs it, and the connection between the squad and the house is close enough that the two calendars are effectively one calendar. Practice clothes on the porch rail, a whiteboard in the hall with routine counts on it, and the largest full length mirror on the Row.

MOO and ARO face each other across the narrowest part of the street, which is why the two porches function as a single social space in warm weather whether either house intended it or not.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 29. Alpha Sigma Sigma (ASS or ΑΣΣ)
- **ID**: _MfchXg2wRDqnBrKR2ff2j

**Description**: Alpha Sigma Sigma. Fraternity house on the outer side of the Row, fourth down, facing Delta Omicron Epsilon.

**Context Description**:
Alpha Sigma Sigma is fourth on the outer side, between Phi Omicron Chi and Beta Epsilon Epsilon, looking across at Delta Omicron Epsilon.

The letters spell ASS and the house has spent decades deciding that this is funny rather than a problem, which has shaped the entire personality of the place. The ASS is the party house of record: loudest, least organised, most likely to be the reason campus security drives down the Row on a Friday.

Vincent and Andrew Campbell both live here.

The alternating layout of the Row puts a sorority directly opposite every fraternity, and the ASS has DOE, which means the house's noise problem is a neighbour problem and not an abstraction.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 30. Mu Alpha Nu (MAN or ΜΑΝ)
- **ID**: _dqQzBhKjnrnLkdD3cyeTF

**Description**: Mu Alpha Nu. Fraternity house at the far south end of the campus side of the Row. The one house with a politics.

**Context Description**:
Mu Alpha Nu is last on the campus side, the furthest house from the main campus and from ARO and the TIT, which is a distance the rest of the Row is quietly glad of. Beta Epsilon Epsilon faces it from the other side of the road.

MAN is not a house so much as a faction. It is where the human students who have a problem with going to a supernatural university end up, and the house closes ranks around hazing that targets demihuman pledges specifically. Tomas Matthews lives here; his father ran a local chapter of Humans First, and the inheritance is not subtle.

Nothing about the building announces this. It is well kept, the lawn is edged, the porch furniture matches. The house presents as the most respectable on the Row and is the one nobody walks past alone at night if they have horns.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 31. Beta Rho Omega (BRO or ΒΡΩ)
- **ID**: _4LEj2zPX66hkh9hG2Fjdz

**Description**: Beta Rho Omega. Fraternity house on the campus side of the Row, immediately south of the TIT. Heavily athletic.

**Context Description**:
Beta Rho Omega is third down the campus side, tucked between Theta Iota Theta above it and Delta Omicron Epsilon below, and looks across the road at Phi Omicron Chi.

The letters read as BRO and the house leans into it without embarrassment. It is the athletes' house: Jared Thompson, Bailey Rogers and Santiago Herrera all live here, and the split between Bulls and Bears runs straight through the common room, which is either the best thing about the house or the reason nothing gets decided in it.

The front yard has a permanent set of weights under a tarp and a barbecue that has never once been put away. Noise carries north to the TIT, which is the single most reliable source of friction on the Row and has never escalated past shouting from a window.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 32. Alpha Rho Omega (ARO or ΑΡΩ)
- **ID**: _xRk2z2UmQ6yhp7ch2yT6e

**Description**: Alpha Rho Omega. Fraternity house at the north end of the campus side of the Row, immediately next door to the TIT.

**Context Description**:
Alpha Rho Omega sits at the very top of the campus side of the Row, first house you reach walking down from the main campus, with Mu Omega Omega directly across the road.

It is the least theatrical of the five fraternities. No columns, no painted letters three feet high, just a wide porch that always has somebody on it and a lawn that gets mowed roughly when somebody remembers. The house reads as a place people live in rather than a place people perform in.

Finnegan Novak lives here. The ARO porch looks straight down onto the Theta Iota Theta front steps, so whoever ends up living in that sorority in a given year is his immediate neighbour and neither of them can leave the house without the possibility of running into the other. Revazhael lives here too, and does the handyman work for the sorority next door, which is less a job than a consequence of geography.

The practical effect of the position is that ARO is the crossing point of the Row. Everyone walking to campus from any of the ten houses passes this porch.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants


### 33. Dullahan's Condo
- **ID**: _YNUQw6fK2rWAd4PrVk7xh

**Description**: A small two-bedroom condo in a beige block a ten minute walk from the SUCC campus, rented under a name that does not appear in any public record. This is where the football coach lives, as far as Solarton is concerned.

THE STATE OF IT: messy in a way that is not neglect so much as absence of any reason to stop. Playbooks and game tape on the coffee table. A weight bench with a cat asleep on it. Three televisions, none of which are ever off. Takeaway boxes. A single framed team photo hung slightly crooked and never straightened. There is a hook-shaped axe leaning against the wall by the door that visitors are told is a prop from a Halloween thing.

THE CATS: too many for the square footage, and Solarton animal control has been called exactly once, by a neighbour who has since moved. Every cat is named after somebody. There is a Barkley. There is a cat named after each of the starting offensive line. He will tell you which is which.

WHO GETS IN: almost nobody. Players have never been inside. Assistant Coach Barkley has, twice, and describes it with the careful vocabulary of a man who does not want to be rude about his boss. The place has no photographs of Dullahan in it, and no mirrors anywhere.

WHY IT MATTERS: this is a cover that became a home. He could live in a set of rooms in voidspace that are objectively better. He rents this instead, and pays the rent on time, in cash, because he likes it here.

**Context Description**:
Dullahan's condo, a small rented two-bedroom in a beige block ten minutes from the SUCC campus. Permanently messy: playbooks, game tape, three televisions always on, takeaway boxes, a weight bench, and a hook-shaped axe by the door he calls a Halloween prop. Far too many cats, each named after somebody he knows. No mirrors and no photographs of himself anywhere. Almost nobody has ever been inside.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 34. Dullahan's Quarters
- **ID**: _Q9wWM3CnDRNEPHL9H6JG7

**Description**: Contractor residences at DDM Headquarters are built to the taste of the occupant, and #04 asked for a house. What he got is a series of rooms in the Contractor wing that open into what looks like a comfortable two-storey home: staircase, landing, kitchen, sitting room, a bathroom with a window that shows a garden nobody has ever been able to reach.

It is filthy. Not squalid, just comprehensively lived in by someone who does not need to sleep and therefore has no reason to ever stop. Weapons on the couch. Takeaway containers from four different realities. A television permanently on. Books face down and open on every surface. Laundry in a chair, and the chair is also a weapon rack.

THE CATS: dozens. Kittens, adult cats, and several things that are cat-adjacent in a way the Anomalous Objects and Creatures department has quietly declined to investigate. Every one of them is named after somebody Dullahan knows, which means there is a Richard, a Luka, a Johan, and at least three Barkleys. He will introduce them by name and will not clarify whether this is a joke.

THE STAFF: two ACES keep the place standing. Bartholomew, a tall and impeccably proper English butler, handles everything that requires dignity. Madge, a portly middle-aged woman in floral aprons, handles the cats and enforces house rules with a severity nobody, including her Contractor, has ever successfully argued with. Shoes off. Nothing bloody on the good carpet.

USE IN PLAY: this is where #04 is when he is not working and not at SUCC. It is the only place he is ever seen genuinely relaxed, and the only place the hood ever comes anywhere near coming off. It never actually does.

**Context Description**:
Dullahan's residence in the Contractor wing of DDM HQ: a set of rooms shaped like a comfortable, permanently messy two-storey house. Weapons on the furniture, a television always on, and dozens of cats, kittens and cat-adjacent creatures, each named after somebody he knows. Kept running by his two ACES, Bartholomew the butler and Madge, who enforces the house rules absolutely.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 35. The Lower Levels
- **ID**: _1EDje1H8c7Yerm4r1nDCD

**Description**: Everything DDM Inc. has ever pulled out of a tear and could not destroy is kept below. The lower levels of Headquarters are the artefact store and the research floors attached to it, and they are the only part of the building that feels like it has weight.

The lighting is worse down here. The corridors are straight, which after the upper floors is its own kind of unsettling. Objects are held in graded containment: sealed cases, warded rooms, and at the deepest level a small number of things kept in rooms that are locked from the inside for reasons the file does not explain.

WHAT IS DOWN THERE: weapons that predate the concept of war. A chair. Recordings of languages that damage the people who transcribe them. Living things classified as objects because no other department would take them. A cabinet of items whose only recorded property is that they were somewhere they should not have been.

ACCESS: restricted. Supernatural Research works these floors under Dr. Johan Jakobsen, and Anomalous Objects and Creatures brings the intake. General staff do not come down without an escort, and are told, honestly, that there is no complete inventory. Items have been logged, moved, and then not found again. The Company's official position is that this is a filing problem.

MOOD: quiet, cold, and full. Nothing down here is trying to get out. Some of it is simply waiting to be picked up.

**Context Description**:
The restricted lower levels of DDM Inc. HQ: the artefact store and attached research floors, worked by Supernatural Research under Dr. Johan Jakobsen. Graded containment from sealed cases to warded rooms locked from the inside. No complete inventory exists. Items have been logged, moved and then not found again; the Company calls this a filing problem.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 36. The Euclidean Division
- **ID**: _yAGh38zJaYQ8BcryfUzKb

**Description**: The department of DDM Inc. responsible for universal logic and physics, headed by Contractor #04, Dullahan. Where the Anomalous Objects and Creatures division deals with things that came through a tear, the Euclidean Division deals with the tear itself, and with everything downstream of it: space that has folded wrong, time that has begun repeating, causality that has come loose, a room whose interior no longer matches its exterior, a staircase with more steps going down than up.

THE OFFICE: a long floor of workstations, whiteboards covered in equations that stop making sense two lines in, and a wall of live monitors tracking flagged anomalies across thousands of realities. Half the desks have coffee rings. One corridor on this floor is a known loop and has been fenced off with hazard tape rather than repaired, because Dullahan finds it funny.

THE HEAD OF DIVISION: Dullahan does not use his office. The chair is stacked with unfiled reports and there are usually at least two cats asleep on the desk. Staff who need him are advised by internal policy to go through an ACE rather than speak to him directly, since prolonged contact with #04 has documented cognitive effects. The policy is politely ignored by everyone who likes him, which is most people.

CULTURE: the Euclidean Division has the best safety record and the worst paperwork compliance in the Company. It also has the departmental record for consecutive Employee of the Month awards, a fact its head has laminated and pinned to the noticeboard.

**Context Description**:
The Euclidean Division of DDM Inc., headed by Contractor #04 Dullahan. Maintains universal logic and physics: detects and repairs spatial, temporal and logical anomalies left behind by reality tears. A long floor of workstations, whiteboards, and monitors tracking anomalies across thousands of realities. One corridor on the floor is a known loop, fenced off with hazard tape rather than fixed.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 37. DDM Inc. Headquarters
- **ID**: _Dmh9d6rcdgyaYVXj1fmFJ

**Description**: Direct Dimensional Management Incorporated keeps its Headquarters in voidspace, which is not a location in any universe. The building has an inside and no outside. Every window is a screen, and every screen shows the same invented skyline at the same permanent hour of the evening. Nobody is fooled. Morale is measurably better with them on.

Space inside HQ does not obey euclidean rules. A corridor can be longer than the wing that contains it. A supply closet can open into a hangar. Some rooms are large enough to have their own weather, and one of them has a permanent low cloud layer that the cleaning staff have named. New employees are given a laminated card explaining that if a hallway loops, they should stop walking and wait.

WHAT IS INSIDE: mess halls and cafeterias that run around the clock, because there is no clock. Training floors, firing ranges, classrooms, libraries, an infirmary that specialises in injuries no mortal doctor has a word for. Entertainment facilities, since immortal staff have to be kept from boredom. Human Resources, which handles recruitment, compliance, psychological support and memory wipes out of the same office.

LIVING QUARTERS: separated into three wings. Mortal staff dormitories. ACE quarters. Contractor residences, each one built to the taste of its occupant and ranging from a medieval hall to a dank cave. ACES are housed as close to their bonded Contractor as the architecture allows, because separation past a day starts to hurt them.

THE LOWER LEVELS: reserved for the storage and study of supernatural artefacts. Restricted. Staff who ask what is down there are told the honest answer, which is that nobody has a complete inventory.

DEPARTMENTS: Anomalous Objects and Creatures. Security and Defense. The Euclidean Division. Supernatural Research. Human Resources. LAZARUS, black ops.

ATMOSPHERE: strip lighting, laminated signage, a break room with a passive-aggressive note about the fridge, and an Employee of the Month board in a building that has no outside. Dullahan's photograph has been on that board for eighteen consecutive years.

**Context Description**:
DDM Inc. Headquarters, a vast non-euclidean building suspended in voidspace with no exterior. Windows are screens faking a cityscape for morale. Contains mess halls, training floors, classrooms, infirmary, HR, and living wings separated for mortal staff, ACES and Contractors. The restricted lower levels store and study supernatural artefacts. Departments: A.O.C, Security and Defense, Euclidean Division, Supernatural Research, Human Resources, LAZARUS.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 38. The Dead Dog Motel
- **ID**: _c76kgyCzWmDU6UxgRh9bq

**Description**: A one-storey roadside motel with a gravel lot, a flickering vending machine and eleven doors. It exists in every reality at once, and in each one it wears a different name. Sleepy Pup Inn. Cur Corpse Tavern. The Last Stop. Locals in each world assume it has always been there, because it has.

The neon sign is the constant. Every version of it shows a dog in the moment of a fatal injury, and every version depicts a different one, because each sign is a portrait of the Original Death of one of the Contractors. Nobody who works the desk will explain this. Nobody who works the desk is entirely a person.

The Motel is the only entrance to DDM Inc. Headquarters available to anyone who is not an employee. The door to voidspace is not marked and is not always the same door. Guests are asked, on a laminated card in every room, to check in promptly and to keep to the corridors. This is not hospitality. The Motel is a liminal space and it presses on the mind of anyone who lingers in it. Visitors who wander lose hours, then names, then the thread of why they came. Staff who have worked there long enough have stopped mentioning it.

DETAILS: fluorescent tube humming over the front desk. Carpet in a pattern that does not repeat correctly. A phone at reception with no dial. Ice machine that runs whether or not anyone is there. The parking lot is always wet, and the rain never falls in front of anyone.

FUNCTION IN PLAY: this is the threshold. Nothing about the Dead Dog Motel is safe, but nothing in it attacks. The danger is duration.

**Context Description**:
The Dead Dog Motel: a liminal roadside motel existing in every reality at once, the only non-employee entrance to DDM Inc. Headquarters in voidspace. Its neon sign always shows a fatally injured dog, a portrait of one Contractor's Original Death. Lingering inside erodes memory and identity. Guests are told to check in promptly.

The sign on the version that opens onto this world shows a dog with no head. That is not decoration and it is not a general symbol of the place. It is Dullahan's Original Death, and the name he took afterwards comes from the same image. He has never commented on it. He also never uses the front entrance if he can avoid it.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 39. Medusa
- **ID**: _LVC8GEhA6KQmTmCwJXhra

**Description**: Hair salon in Bricklane, next door to the Yeti Shack. Cuts hair whichever part of you it happens to grow from.

**Context Description**:
Medusa has a narrow street front on the main Bricklane lane, between the Yeti Shack and a shop that has been closing down for three years. Mirror, warm light, six chairs, and a painted sign that has been repainted so many times the snakes have changed style twice.

It is a hair salon, and the whole point of it is in the line printed on the window: whichever part of you your hair may grow from. Head hair, obviously. Also manes, ruffs, tail plumes, the coat down a lycan's forearms in partial shift, feathers that need shaping rather than cutting, the fur behind ears that do not lie flat. Fur trimming and shedding season deshedding are a bigger part of the revenue than haircuts.

The staff are trained on non human hair and do not flinch, which sounds small and is not. They know which products go near skin that reacts to silver, which clients cannot sit under a dryer, and which ones need the chair moved because of a tail. Booking is by phone or by walking in, and the wait is longer in the week before formals.

The permanent handwritten sign taped to the till reads WE HAVE SEEN WORSE.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants

**Marketplace**: Shop

### 54. Bricklane Mall
- **ID**: _Upt6xefBdbXMjJwWqf7Pe

**Description**: Solarton's open air shopping village, a cluster of low independent storefronts on their own lane grid, pressed up against the east side of Solarton Square.

**Context Description**:
Bricklane is not a mall in any sense a visitor from a bigger city would recognise. It is a quaint outdoor shopping area of low two storey buildings, each one its own business with its own front door onto the street, laid out on a small grid of lanes too narrow for through traffic. Cars stop at the edge and everyone walks.

The paving is old red brick, which is where the name comes from, worn smooth in the middle of each lane and still sharp at the kerbs. Two planted courtyards break the grid, one with a fountain that works most of the year and one with three jacarandas and a ring of benches that fills up between classes. String lights cross the lanes overhead and stay up year round.

The tenants are the ordinary business of a coastal college town, sized for a population that is not entirely human. Nothing here is a chain. Shops open late in term time and close early in August. Students treat it as the default place to be when there is nowhere they have to be, which means most conversations in Solarton that are not on campus happen somewhere in these four lanes.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants

**Marketplace**: Info-point (1 items)

L'assenza di simbologia fissa permanente la rende adattabile a qualunque tipo di rito o celebrazione. ### 69. Theta Iota Theta (TIT or ΘΙΘ)
- **ID**: _efKWhLjb81zqdyYDTHQbe

**Description**: Theta Iota Theta. Casa da party, informale e un po' disastrata, popolare fra le succubi. Sul lato campus della Row, fra ARO e BRO, di fronte alla KSA.

**Context Description**:
Theta Iota Theta sta seconda sul lato campus della Row, fra Alpha Rho Omega sopra e Beta Rho Omega sotto, e guarda Kappa Sigma Alpha dall'altra parte della strada.

E' la casa da party per reputazione e la meno formale delle cinque sorority per abitudine. Informale, un po' disastrata, con un portico che regge piu' peso di quanto dovrebbe e un impianto elettrico su cui Revazhael, che sta ad ARO, mette le mani almeno una volta al mese.

E' popolare fra le succubi, e fra le fraternity, il che e' la stessa frase detta due volte.

La posizione e' la sua particolarita': la TIT e' l'unica casa della Row con una fraternity sopra, una sotto e una di fronte, quindi e' il punto in cui l'alternanza maschile-femminile della strada isola chi ci abita. Chiunque ci viva ha tre porticati di ragazzi entro trenta metri e nessuna sorority a portata di voce.

Sierra e Scarlett Rose ci abitano e sono coinquiline.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 70. Kappa Sigma Alpha (KSA or ΚΣΑ)
- **ID**: _H3gdXqj8YyXYpxBtnBeYe

**Description**: Kappa Sigma Alpha. Casa storica e opulenta dei licantropi dominata da Noah. Territorio de-facto del branco Bloodmoon nel campus. Odore pesante di Alpha pack. L'ingresso è strettamente riservato ai membri o agli invitati approvati da Noah.

**Context Description**:


KSA e' la casa di famiglia dei Douglas da tre generazioni. Erik Douglas ne e' stato presidente, Logan e Malachia sono alumni, e oggi Noah ne e' presidente e occupa la President's Suite. La scheda di Logan lo formula come una regola e non come un'abitudine: come ogni maschio Douglas, e' stato iniziato a KSA. Erik si aspetta che tocchi a Jasper, e Noah lo spingera' a fare la rush. Jasper non vuole, e il precedente di Logan, che e' entrato e poi se n'e' andato, e' vivo e cena a casa.

Sulla Row, KSA sta sul lato esterno in seconda posizione, esattamente di fronte alla Theta Iota Theta (TIT or ΘΙΘ). Dalla casa che presiede, Noah vede la porta della Theta Iota Theta.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants

### 72. Wyrm Dormitories
- **ID**: _L3e2jJCR3LbVTJUP1Yaxh

**Description**: I dormitori principali del SUCC. Ali divise in stanze per diurni e notturni, dotate di pareti insonorizzate e distributori della banca del sangue. Ala Nord: studenti umani. Ali Ovest ed Est: stanze miste. Ala Sud: ala soprannaturale. Palestra riservata agli studenti, parcheggio a nord, strutture speciali (stanze sole/luna, banca del sangue).

**Context Description**:
Il complesso dei Wyrm Dormitories vive due ritmi paralleli: di giorno l'ala Sud è silenziosa e le tapparelle restano abbassate, mentre le ali Nord, Ovest ed Est brulicano della vita studentesca umana e mista abituale. Di notte lo schema si inverte. Le pareti insonorizzate rendono possibile la convivenza tra i due ritmi senza troppi attriti, e la palestra riservata ai residenti resta uno dei pochi spazi comuni frequentati da entrambe le popolazioni a qualunque ora.

Edificio moderno e squadrato, il più recente del campus. Gli alloggi sono a suite: due o tre camere che condividono bagno e un piccolo soggiorno comune, e le suite sono miste, uomini e donne insieme, senza alcuna separazione per sesso in nessuna delle quattro ali. Chi arriva da una casa tradizionale ci mette qualche settimana ad abituarsi, e ogni settembre qualche genitore fa una domanda in proposito alla segreteria.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants

### 75. Nocturnal Hall
- **ID**: _mF8QcK9DLBAX2rBXq4VNJ

**Description**: Edificio del SUCC senza finestre, con illuminazione artificiale flebile e temperatura controllata. Dedicato alle lezioni per gli studenti notturni (vampiri, ombre, ecc).

**Context Description**:
Durante le ore notturne l'edificio ospita le lezioni riservate agli studenti notturni (vampiri, ombre e simili), grazie all'illuminazione artificiale flebile e alla temperatura rigidamente controllata. Durante il giorno, quando i corsi notturni sono sospesi, gli stessi spazi vengono informalmente riconvertiti in un club/salotto studentesco, frequentato anche da chi non segue quei corsi ma cerca un ambiente tranquillo e in penombra. È inoltre una delle due sedi (a rotazione stagionale con il Lunar Quad) di The Pack, la società ufficiale were e canide/lupina del SUCC, che si riunisce il martedì dalle 19 alle 21 come sistema di supporto per chi fatica, accademicamente o per motivi legati alla propria natura.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 76. Building A
- **ID**: _wKqnnMGAGFgFMz1jqEJFU

**Description**: Aule generiche del campus, frequentate per lo più da umani e per corsi base.

**Context Description**:
L'edificio raggruppa sia aule generiche di piccole dimensioni sia lecture theatres più capienti, usate soprattutto per i corsi introduttivi e le classi a maggioranza umana. È il primo punto di contatto con il campus per molte matricole umane, per lo stile architettonico più familiare rispetto agli edifici magici del resto del campus.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 77. Basilisk Library
- **ID**: _V7c6QkdPg93E8Y8Rembp6

**Description**: La colossale biblioteca del SUCC, con sezioni dedicate a tomi mondani e archivi magici protetti.

**Context Description**:
La Basilisk Library si sviluppa su cinque piani. Il piano terra ospita la sezione mondana, con tomi accademici standard e sale lettura aperte a tutti; i piani superiori custodiscono l'archivio magico protetto, ad accesso riservato e sorvegliato con mezzi anche magici. L'edificio offre numerose study rooms insonorizzate, prenotabili per lo studio individuale o di gruppo, e una Trophy Room che espone i cimeli sportivi e accademici della storia del SUCC. L'ampio portico d'ingresso, coperto, è un punto di ritrovo comune nei giorni di pioggia e un frequente rifugio informale tra una lezione e l'altra. Il seminterrato ospita la Meeting Room 005, sede fissa dell'Anime Club (il venerdì alle 18, ritrovo informale aperto a studenti umani e soprannaturali appassionati di anime e manga). Alcune sale della biblioteca sono inoltre climatizzate a temperature più elevate del normale, pensate per accogliere studenti e personale a sangue freddo durante l'inverno.

Dettaglio dal portale ufficiale: la biblioteca ha stanze riscaldate riservate a studenti e personale a sangue freddo, che altrimenti non riuscirebbero a lavorarci per piu' di un'ora. Sono le sale piu' contese dell'edificio in inverno, e non solo dai rettili.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 78. Unicorn Hall
- **ID**: _zkBnf99R4feq97ytYd1V4

**Description**: Edificio del SUCC progettato con corridoi immensi e porte doppie per essere accessibile a creature quadrupedi, alate o di stazza fuori norma.

**Context Description**:
Oltre ai corridoi sovradimensionati e alle porte doppie pensate per creature quadrupedi, alate o di stazza fuori norma, l'edificio ospita La Serra Xenobotanica (Greenhouse), usata sia per i corsi di botanica applicata sia come spazio di sosta tranquillo per studenti di grandi dimensioni o dotati di ali, che vi trovano più spazio e comfort rispetto alle aule standard. Unicorn Hall è anche la sede fissa del BigFeet Hiking Club, il gruppo (popolare tra i cryptid) che organizza escursioni, campeggi e la tradizionale gita settimanale estiva.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants

Un Auditorium interno viene usato regolarmente per saggi, spettacoli studenteschi e proiezioni.

Il quartiere ospita i dipartimenti di scienze esatte e applicate, con edifici moderni e austeri dedicati a fisica, chimica e ingegneria. Al suo interno si trova Vault 4, il reparto di Specimen Containment ad altissima sicurezza, dove vengono custoditi ed esaminati campioni biologici e magici, comuni e rari, sotto stretta sorveglianza e accesso rigidamente controllato.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

### 82. Griffin Clocktower
- **ID**: _JxQpqR1cJAcgaw8khJxDx

**Description**: La torre dell'orologio che domina il campus SUCC. Punto di ritrovo iconico.

**Context Description**:
Oltre al celebre orologio e alle gargoyle a forma di grifone che si animano e sorvolano la torre ogni ora, l'edificio ospita ai piani superiori le Lecture Theatres delle facoltà di Giurisprudenza e Ingegneria, insieme agli uffici del corpo docente. Nel seminterrato, isolato dal resto della torre e accessibile principalmente in orario serale e notturno, si trova The Pendulum, un piccolo club informale, punto di ritrovo abituale per gli studenti dei corsi notturni di Nocturnal Hall. La torre, che di fatto funge da principale struttura per le lezioni del campus, chiude periodicamente per manutenzione: durante queste chiusure le classi vengono temporaneamente ricollocate a Building C.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants


### 83. Archer Wolfwood Hall
- **ID**: _HwtQ1Rbb81Xn78J9CGC6M

**Description**: Edificio accademico storico del SUCC Campus. Struttura "Magic-Proofed" resistente a esplosioni ed evocazioni accidentali.

**Context Description**:
È la principale sala conferenze del campus, magic-proofed per resistere a esplosioni ed evocazioni accidentali durante dimostrazioni pratiche o incidenti di laboratorio. Ospita dozzine di aule e teatri, usati sia per lezioni frontali sia per assemblee e presentazioni a tutto il campus, come l'assemblea inaugurale dell'anno accademico.

L'edificio porta il nome del Dean Archer Wolfwood, il rettore che nel 2002 ha aperto SUCC agli studenti umani. È la decisione che ha definito l'ateneo, ed è anche l'origine della rivalità con CUMS, che quella strada non l'ha mai presa.

**Timeline Window**: start to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants

### 92. Yarrow River
- **ID**: _2C4xGHAVGqr9DFdt4P2tB

**Description**: The river that rises under the sacred yew in Bloodmoon territory, runs past Blackwood City down to Dockside, and reaches the sea near Solarton.

**Context Description**:
The Yarrow rises at the foot of the sacred yew that Wulfnic planted, in the deep north of Blackwood Forest, as a spring that comes up cold between the roots and never runs dry. For the first miles it is a forest stream, narrow enough to step over in places, dark with tannin and shaded the whole way.

It leaves the forest along the northern edge of Seven Hills, widens, and runs down the flank of Blackwood City to Dockside, where it is deep enough for small craft and lined with old stone quays. Below Dockside it slows across the coastal flats and reaches the sea a short way north of Solarton.

The pack considers the whole length of it theirs in a way they do not bother to explain to anyone, and the water is treated as clean regardless of what any municipal test says, because of where it starts.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants

### 96. Passenger Ferry Solarton Line
- **ID**: _gbgeEj8MBPkwFTFCKYqTd

**Description**: The passenger ferry that runs the Yarrow between Blackwood and the coast. Three stops: Dockside, Hex Valley, Solarton.

**Context Description**:
The line runs the navigable stretch of the Yarrow, from the landing at Dockside in Blackwood, down through Hex Valley where it calls at the jetty below the vineyards, to the terminal at the river mouth in Solarton's Bay Area.

One boat, wooden, flat bottomed for the shallow reaches, with an open upper deck and a saloon below for the weather. It carries passengers, bicycles and a fair amount of freight that would cost more by road. Three departures a day in summer, one in winter, and none at all when the river is low.

The whole run takes most of a day in either direction, which is slower than the 101 by a wide margin. People take it because of what the river passes through: the forest edge, the flank of Blackwood, the terraces of the valley, and the widening mouth at the end. It is the only way to see the three places as one place.

It is also the only scheduled service that crosses all three jurisdictions without a road, which is not lost on anybody who has a reason to prefer that.

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 3 variants

### 114. The Verve
- **ID**: _M6xM1hn9NJfPPEGb8zcdz
- **Avatar**: https://imagedelivery.net/Dv4koOwHQU3XnXLqtl0aVQ/2e8063db-a6fd-4940-dad7-25329cce3a00/public

**Description**: Logan Douglas's garage by day, exclusive underground nightclub by night, and the one place in the family's territory that answers to nobody but him.

**Context Description**:
808 Neon Avenue, Bluemoon. Legally declared Neutral Territory, a status it shares only with Sidewinders Bar and the SUCC Campus.

By day it's an exposed-brick industrial space with arched windows framing the Blackwood City skyline. A genuine working garage bay with a two-post lift takes up one side, tools racked on red toolboxes, a motorcycle parked near a neon "Gasoline Filling Station" sign. The other side opens into a long bar serving blue-collar regulars who drift in for a beer between shifts, worn leather couches, a low wooden coffee table. The decor is Logan's quiet, unspoken tribute to Nixara: vintage "Bloodmoon Motor Oil" signage and a neon reading "The Bloodmoon" hang over the bar. Nobody who doesn't already know asks why.

After hours, a hydraulic lift system raises the entire workshop bay, car and all, up into the mezzanine, retracting tools and benches out of the way. A dance floor and stage drop down from above in their place, and The Verve becomes an exclusive underground nightclub.

Above it all, reachable by a service stairwell, is Logan's penthouse loft, home to him and Edric.

The entire building sits inside Logan's jamming field: no DCC surveillance, no drones, no biometric tracking works inside.

**Tags**: garage, nightclub, neutral territory, Logan Douglas, no surveillance

**Timeline Window**: 10323648 to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 0 to 5 variants

**Final Instructions**:
Day: grounded, working-class, unpretentious, mechanical sounds and motor oil. Night: neon-lit, music-driven, exclusive-but-unsnobby energy. In both modes, this is the one place where characters can speak freely, no surveillance, no pack politics enforced. Let dialogue here be more honest and less guarded than anywhere else in the story.

**Context Description**:
Bluemoon is Blackwood City's nightlife district, a former industrial strip of exposed brick warehouses and converted garages now split between working-class daytime businesses and after-dark venues. By day it's quiet, mechanics, diners, corner bars serving locals. After sundown, neon signage flickers on across the district and it becomes the city's underground entertainment corridor, drawing a mixed crowd of humans and supernaturals alike. Several venues here operate under Neutral Territory status, making Bluemoon one of the few parts of the city where faction politics are expected to stay outside.

### 116. Lunar Quad
- **ID**: _EXgBp6GLC7X2fB2btYMDF

**Description**: The central quad of SUCC. Everyone crosses it, all day long.

**Context Description**:
Il piazzale centrale del SUCC, incrocio nevralgico della vita studentesca. Affollato a tutte le ore.

Prato calpestato e cemento chiaro accecante sotto il sole, gruppi sparsi sulle panchine e sull'erba, biciclette abbandonate ovunque. Il chiacchiericcio sovrapposto di decine di specie diverse, qualcuno che ripassa ad alta voce, qualcuno che dorme sullo zaino. Da qui si vede la Griffin Clocktower e si raggiunge tutto il resto del campus.

Nelle sere in cui non si tiene a Nocturnal Hall (a rotazione stagionale), il Lunar Quad ospita anche le riunioni del martedì di The Pack, la società ufficiale were e canide/lupina del campus.

Al centro del piazzale c'è la fontana decorativa della luna. La sua faccia riproduce sempre la fase lunare corrente, quindi cambia di notte in notte, ed è il modo più veloce che ha chiunque attraversi il campus per sapere a che punto è il mese. Nei giorni intorno alla luna piena il piazzale si riempie molto oltre il solito.

**Timeline Window**: 9469608 to end hours

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 50 variants

**Final Instructions**:
Luogo di passaggio e di incontri casuali. Qualcuno di conosciuto può sempre comparire qui senza bisogno di spiegazioni.

Spazio pubblico e visibile: le conversazioni private qui vengono sentite.

**Description**: The Douglas ancestral estate. Home, fortress, and the most watched building in Blackwood.

**Context Description**:
La residenza ancestrale dei Douglas al 555 Oak Road, dentro Seven Hills. Casa e fortezza insieme: profondamente amata, costantemente monitorata, completamente soffocante.

Cuoio tattico freddo, cedro, mogano lucidato e un sentore lontano di carne cruda. Luce pesante e opulenta di caminetto. Il peso opprimente del calore corporeo di corpi da licantropo adulto nelle stanze affollate. La ventilazione è progettata per gestire gli odori e isolare l'aggressività prima che si propaghi. Le porte sono enormi, dimensionate per gli Hybrid Shift. Dal fondo dell'ala est arriva il vapore profumato della Pack Bathhouse termale comune.

Dal retro della proprietà parte il sentiero che sale nei boschi fino al territorio di Wulfnic e alla longhouse. È sterrato, stretto, non segnalato e non asfaltato, e non ne esiste un secondo: chi va dal branco dei Firstborn passa da qui.

## Scenarios (3)

### 1. Ciao, Sono Logan
- **ID**: _EyeRqnmmcc89mewWEMVLp

**Description**: Venerdì 20 settembre 2024, quarta settimana di college. Alyssa divide la stanza con Scarlett da agosto, e le tre escono per la prima volta davvero: i Grave Mistake suonano al Sidewinders. Erik ha dato il permesso soltanto perché Logan si è offerto come accompagnatore, dato che a un concerto indie punk uno come lui dà molto meno nell'occhio di Kaladin o di Malachia. Mac Sanchez-Rogers, tastierista e convinto di essere stato la star della serata, individua una ragazza rimasta sola al bancone e va a fare la sua scena. Non ha notato l'uomo a otto sgabelli di distanza.

**Insertion Point**: 10490519 hours

**Character Capacity**: 1 to 8 variants

**Included Characters** (8):
  - Character ID: _rm9PkkbWEc1zNJAUNUp6U, Weight: 100%
  - Character ID: _gYwk8EbFrtwPatB9CgC3D, Weight: 90%
  - Character ID: _qpVJWG3jnxAbwBmJmEttB, Weight: 0%
  - Character ID: _brTj3Xgk9R2YPMYbKYCNh, Weight: 70%
  - Character ID: _c2ARRyzmbCdEhrwbHWgFr, Weight: 70%
  - Character ID: _93KXYePUrXYrMPWnkDVLx, Weight: 50%
  - Character ID: _H84mBnGcVWwX6DFCqY6f2, Weight: 50%
  - Character ID: _QdB3BNtd3X6Dzrb196PXy, Weight: 30%

**Premade Scenes** (1):

**Template 1**: Sidewinders, dopo il set
```
=>Narrator:
Venerdì 20 settembre 2024, 23:00. Sidewinders Bar & Nightclub, Solarton.

Sono quattro settimane che i gemelli sono a SUCC, e sono quattro settimane che Alyssa divide una stanza con Scarlett. Stasera è la prima vera uscita delle tre: i Grave Mistake suonavano al Sidewinders, e una di loro ha deciso che ci si andava.

Erik ha detto di sì soltanto perché Logan si è offerto di accompagnarle. A un concerto indie punk uno come Logan dà molto meno nell'occhio di Kaladin o di Malachia, ed è tutto il motivo per cui questa serata esiste.

La band ha finito da venti minuti e il locale non si è ancora svuotato. Il pavimento appiccica sotto le suole. L'aria sa di birra rovesciata, sudore soprannaturale e ozono. Qualcuno fischia ancora verso un palco dove non c'è più nessuno.

Scarlett e Sierra sono sparite verso il bagno da cinque minuti, di quelli che al bagno diventano quindici. Alyssa è rimasta al bancone.

All'altro capo del bancone, a otto sgabelli di distanza, con una birra che non ha quasi toccato, c'è Logan. Ha passato tutta la serata esattamente lì: abbastanza lontano da non farla sentire sorvegliata, abbastanza vicino da vedere tutto. In un posto così, uno come lui non lo guarda nessuno. È il punto.

=>Mac:
Mac sta galleggiando. Letteralmente galleggiando, su una nuvola fatta di adrenalina, di birra scadente bevuta prima del concerto e delle urla di un pubblico a cui è piaciuto. A cui è piaciuto lui.

Cazzo, li abbiamo distrutti.

Anche Fade non ha crepato sugli acuti, per una volta. E Via ha spaccato, va detto. Ma Mac sa benissimo chi fosse la vera star della serata, e adesso, gonfio della luce del dopo concerto, gli serve soltanto qualcuno che glielo confermi.

L'ha vista durante il secondo pezzo. Difficile non vederla, in mezzo a quella gente. E Mac è certo che lo stesse guardando: non Via con le sue acrobazie, non Fade che fa il re vampiro imbronciato, non Roland che marcisce dietro ai tamburi. Lui. Ogni volta che colpiva un accordo le è andato a cercare gli occhi, e se n'è assicurato. Non gli è passato per la testa nemmeno per un secondo che una a quel concerto potesse guardare il palco e basta.

Passa in mezzo a un gruppo di satiri che si lamenta dei prezzi dei drink, e non si scusa quando ne urta uno abbastanza forte da fargli rovesciare mezza pinta. Sotto la birra e il sudore c'è qualcos'altro, qualcosa di dolce che non riesce a collocare. Mac non ha la più pallida idea di cosa stia annusando. Ha deciso che vuol dire di sì.

Ed eccola. Da sola. Perfetto.

Mac si infila nello spazio accanto a lei, invadendolo di proposito. Incombe. Sa di essere grosso, spalle larghe, braccia ancora lucide di sudore, e lo usa: si piazza in modo da toglierle mezzo locale dalla visuale. Fa cenno al barista per una birra, poi le dà tutta la sua attenzione.

"Allora." Abbassa la voce di un'altra ottava. "Me lo dici che sono stato pazzesco, o devo indovinare?"

Si appoggia con il gomito al bancone appiccicoso, chiudendola contro il bordo. La coda scodinzola e ha già comunicato tutto il comunicabile.

"Ti ho beccata a fissarmi, tesoro." Si stiracchia, fingendo di essere rilassato. "Mi chiamo Mac. Ma probabilmente lo sapevi già."

"Ce l'hai un nome, o resti lì seduta a farti guardare?"

=>Logan:
Otto sgabelli più in là, Logan posa la birra sul bancone. Non la sbatte. La posa, e il rumore basta lo stesso.

Si schiarisce la gola.

Poi arriva, senza nessuna fretta, mani in tasca, e si ferma dall'altro lato di Alyssa. Non in mezzo ai due. Di fianco. Guarda Mac con l'espressione di uno che sta ascoltando un motore e ha già capito da dove perde.

"Ciao." Una pausa lunga esattamente quanto serve. "Sono Logan."

Beve un sorso.

"Suo zio."
```
